का.आ. 2164.--केन्द्र सरकार, राजभाषा (संघ के शासकीय प्रयोजनों के लिए प्रयोग) नियम,1976 के नियम 10 
के उप-नियम (4) के अनुसरण में राजस्व विभाग के केन्द्रीय अप्रत्यक्ष कर एवं सीमा शुल्क बोर्ड के अधीन केन्द्रीय माल एवं 
सेवा कर (अपील) आयुक्तालय, गुरुग्राम के कार्यालय, जिसके 80 प्रतिशत से अधिक अधिकारियों एवं कर्मचारियों ने हिन्दी 
का कार्यसाधक ज्ञान प्राप्त कर लिया है, को अधिसूचित करती है। 


[फा. सं. ई-11017/3/2017- हिन्दी-2-अधिसूचना]

अधिकारियों/ कर्मचारियों ने हिंदी का कार्यसाधक ज्ञान प्राप्त कर लिया है, को एतदद्वारा अधिसूचित करता है:-

[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7525 
पेट्रोलियम एवं प्राकृतिक गैस मंत्रालय 
नई दिल्‍ली, 16 दिसम्बर, 2025 


का.आ. 2170.--केन्द्रीय सरकार ने पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 
अधिनियम, 1962 (1962 का 50) की धारा 6 की उपधारा (1) के अधीन जारी की गई भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम एवं 
प्राकृतिक गैस मंत्रालय की नीचे दी गई अनुसूची में यथा उल्लिखित तारीखों की एवम का. आ. द्वारा उन अधिसूचनाओं से 
संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन किया था| 


और केन्द्रीय सरकार ने, उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा (4) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, 
उक्त भूमियों में, सभी विल्लंगमों से मुक्त, उपयोग का अधिकार आई एच बी लिमिटेड में निहित किया था| 

और सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने केन्द्रीय सरकार को रिपोर्ट दी है कि पेट्रोलियम उत्पाद के परिवहन के लिए गुजरात राज्य 
में आई एच बी लिमिटेड की कांडला गोरखपुर एल.पी.जी. पाइपलाइन बिछाई जा चुकी हैं, अतः उस भूमि के बारे में, 
जिसका संक्षिप्त विवरण इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची से विनिर्दिष्ट किया गया हैं, ऐसे प्रचालन को गुजरात राज्य में 
समाप्त किया जाए| 

अतः अब, केन्द्रीय सरकार, पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 1963 के 
नियम 4 के स्पष्टीकरण - 1 के अधीन उस तारीख को जिन पर यह अधिसूचना भारत के राजपत्र में प्रकाशित होती हैं, 


गुजरात राज्य के पंचमहाल जिले के नीचे वर्णित गाँवो में मार्गाधिकार गतिविधियों की समाप्ती की तारीख के रूप में 
घोषित करती हैं | 


अनुसूची

का.आ. 2171.--केन्द्रीय सरकार ने पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 
अधिनियम, 1962 (1962 का 50) की धारा 6 की उपधारा (1) के अधीन जारी की गई भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम एवं 
प्राकृतिक गैस मंत्रालय की नीचे दी गई अनुसूची में यथा उल्लिखित तारीखों की एवम का. आ. द्वारा उन अधिसूचनाओं से 
संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन किया था| 


और केन्द्रीय सरकार ने, उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा (4) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, 
उक्त भूमियों में, सभी विल्लंगमों से मुक्त, उपयोग का अधिकार आई एच बी लिमिटेड में निहित किया था| 

और सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने केन्द्रीय सरकार को रिपोर्ट दी है कि पेट्रोलियम उत्पाद के परिवहन के लिए गुजरात राज्य 
में आई एच बी लिमिटेड की कांडला गोरखपुर एल.पी.जी. पाइपलाइन बिछाई जा चुकी हैं, अतः उस भूमि के बारे में, 
जिसका संक्षिप्त विवरण इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची से विनिर्दिष्ट किया गया हैं, ऐसे प्रचालन को गुजरात राज्य में 
समाप्त किया जाए| 

अतः अब, केन्द्रीय सरकार, पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 1963 के 
नियम 4 के स्पष्टीकरण - 1 के अधीन उस तारीख को जिन पर यह अधिसूचना भारत के राजपत्र में प्रकाशित होती हैं, 


गुजरात राज्य के वडोदरा जिले के नीचे वर्णित गाँवो में मार्गाधिकार गतिविधियों की समाप्ती की तारीख के रूप में घोषित 
करती हैं |

का.आ. 2172.--केन्द्रीय सरकार ने पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 
अधिनियम, 1962 (1962 का 50) की धारा 6 की उपधारा (1) के अधीन जारी की गई भारत सरकार के पेट्रोलियम एवं 
प्राकृतिक गैस मंत्रालय की नीचे दी गई अनुसूची में यथा उल्लिखित तारीखों की एवम का. आ. द्वारा उन अधिसूचनाओं से 
संलग्न अनुसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन किया था| 

और केन्द्रीय सरकार ने, उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 6 की उपधारा (4) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, 
उक्त भूमियों में, सभी विल्लंगमों से मुक्त, उपयोग का अधिकार आई एच बी लिमिटेड में निहित किया था| 

और सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने केन्द्रीय सरकार को रिपोर्ट दी है कि पेट्रोलियम उत्पाद के परिवहन के लिए गुजरात राज्य 
में आई एच बी लिमिटेड की कांडला गोरखपुर एल.पी.जी. पाइपलाइन बिछाई जा चुकी हैं, अतः उस भूमि के बारे में, 
जिसका संक्षिप्त विवरण इस अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनुसूची से विनिर्दिष्ट किया गया हैं, ऐसे प्रचालन को गुजरात राज्य में 
समाप्त किया जाए| 

अतः अब, केन्द्रीय सरकार, पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन (भूमि में उपयोग के अधिकार का अर्जन) 1963 के 
नियम 4 के स्पष्टीकरण - 1 के अधीन उस तारीख को जिन पर यह अधिसूचना भारत के राजपत्र में प्रकाशित होती हैं, 
गुजरात राज्य के दाहोद जिले के नीचे वर्णित गाँवो में मार्गाधिकार गतिविधियों की समाप्ती की तारीख के रूप में घोषित 
करती हैं | 


अनुसूची 
जिला: दाहोद राज्य : गुजरात

का.आ. 2173.---औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण - सह - श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल के पंचाट(एलसी/आर-20/2017) को 
प्रकाशित करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 15/12,/2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. एल-22012/43/2017-आईआर(सी.एम-1)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 


MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND EMLOYMENT 
New Delhi, the 16th December, 2025 


S.O. 2173.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference.LC/R/20/2017) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management 
of ECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 15/12/2025. 


[No. L-22012/43/2017 - IR (CM-ID)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL. 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 
Presiding Officer, 
C.G.I.T-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 20 OF 2017 
PARTIES: Bhusan Agharia 
Vs. 
Management of Bejdih Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 


For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7535 


INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 22.10.2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/43/2017-IR(CM-II) dated 11.12.2017 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Bejdih Colliery under Sodepur Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“ Whether the action of the management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. In relation to its Bejdih Colliery 
under Sodepur Area in imposing a punishment of dismissal on Shri Bushan Agharia. UG Loader w.e,f. 
03/05.08.1998, vide order No BMP/C46/B-12/469 dated 03/05.08.1998 is just and legal? If not, to what relief 
the workman is entitled to? ” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22012/43/2017-IR(CM-ID dated 11.12.2017 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 19.12.2017 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Union filed written statement for the dismissed workman on 08.11.2021. Fact of the case as disclosed in their 
written statement in brief is that, Bhusan Agharia was a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited 
(hereinafterreferred to as ECL), posted at Bejdih Colliery under Sodepur Area as an Underground Loader, bearing 
U.M. No. 138198. Due to illness he could not attend his duty from 04.04.1996. After recovery he reported for his duty 
and requested the management to allow him to join duty but the management did not allow him to join and issued a 
Charge Sheet bearing No. 115 dated 21.08.1996 alleging misconduct on account of his unauthorized absence, 
violating provision of Section 17(i)(n) of Model Standing Orders. After a long gap management issued a second 
Charge Sheet dated 16.03.1998 without mentioning the date of absence from duty. Management did not take action on 
the basis of earlier Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 and conducted enquiry on the basis of the second Charge Sheet 
dated 16.03.1998. According to the workman his total period of absence from duty is four month and sixteen days 
from 04.04.1996 to 21.08.1996 but management without servicing the Charge Sheet and Notice of enquiry held ex- 
parte proceeding. No second Show Cause Notice was issued to him which is a gross violation of the decision of the 
Hon'ble Supreme Court of India as well as the guidelines issued by the Director (P&IR), Coal India Limited. Further 
contention of the union is that Bhusan Agharia was having difficulty to work as an Underground Loader which is a 
hard and hazardous job. He requested the management for deploying him at some other place to perform time rated 
job but management did not consider his request. The workman succumbed to illness and was compelled to remain 
absent from duty. The management of ECL without providing any opportunity to the workman to respond to the 
findings of the Enquiry Officer dismissed him from service. Union has challenged the punishment of dismissal as 
illegal, arbitrary and disproportionate to the nature of misconduct. After his dismissal the workman submitted two 
applications dated 04.07.2012 and 28.06.2013 for his reinstatement as per terms of Memorandum of Settlement dated 
22.05.2007 but the management did not consider the same. 


3. Management of ECL in their written statement filed on 26.04.2023 submitted that the workman absented 
from duty from 04.04.1996 to 10.03.1998, for a period of one year nine months and twelve days without any 
information or sanctioned leave as such he was chargesheeted on 16.03.1998 under clause 17(i)(n) of the Model 
Standing Order for his unauthorized absence from duty. The Enquiry Officer was appointed to conduct enquire into 
the charge. Enquiry Officer issued Notice of enquiry to the workman on 06.04.1998, 10.06.1998 and 04.07.1998 but 
the workman did not participate and the enquiry was held ex-parte. The charge was established against the workman 
and the Disciplinary Authority dismissed the workman from service w.e.f. 05.08.1998. The order was communicated 
to the workman. Management claimed that the punishment of dismissal is proportionate to the misconduct and the 
workman is not entitled to any relief. 


4. The short point for consideration is whether dismissal of Bhusan Agharia from his service is just and legal. If 
not, what relief he is entitled to. 


5. The union examined Bhusan Agharia as Workman Witness No. 1 and filed his affidavit-in-chief. In his 
affidavit-in-chief the witness stated that after issuing Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 he submitted his reply and 
requested the management to allow him to join duty but in spite of repeated request management neither allowed him 
to join duty nor held any enquiry on the basis of the Charge Sheet. The management of ECL thereafter issued another 
Charge Sheet dated 16.03.1998 with a charge that he did not perform his duty in the year 1997. The workman witness 
averred that after issuance of Charge Sheet on 21.08.1996 he was not allowed to join his duty therefore it was not 


7536 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


possible for him to perform duty in the year 1997 which is the charge disclosed in the subsequent Charge Sheet. He 
stated that the total period of absence was four 


months and sixteen days only and the management did not serve any Notice of enquiry to him and proceeded to hold 
ex-parte enquiry. Furthermore, no second Show Cause Notice was issued to him before dismissal. During his 
examination-in-chief the workman produced the following documents : 


(i) A Copy of the Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 is produced as Exhibit W-1. 
(ii) Copy of the reply to the Charge Sheet submitted on 23.08.1996 by the workman, as Exhibit W-2. 


(ili) Copy of the Mercy Petitions submitted on 04.07.2012 and 28.06.2013, as Exhibit W-3 and W-4 
respectively. 


6. In course of cross-examination the workman witness stated that he remained absent due to his illness but 
expressed his inability to produce any document in support of his illness or medical treatment during the period of his 
absence. He denied the suggestion that he intentionally remained absent from duty and further denied that he 
intentionally remained away from the enquiry proceeding. 


7. Mr. Apurva Saurabh Singh, Assistant Manager (Personnel), Bejdih Colliery has been examined as 
Management Witness No. 1. He filed an affidavit-in-chief wherein he stated that Bhusan Agharia had remained absent 
from duty from 04.04.1996 to 21.08.1996 for a period of four months without any information and sanctioned leave. 
He further stated that a domestic enquiry was held on the Charge and Enquiry Officer issued Notices to the workman 
on 06.04.1998, 10.06.1998 and 04.07.1998 but the workman did not attend the enquiry, as a result enquiry was held 
ex-parte. Management witness stated that the workman was dismissed from service w.e.f. 05.08.1998 and his 
dismissal is fully justified. 


In course of evidence the management witness produced the following documents: 
(i) Copy of the Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 is produced as Exhibit M-1. 
(ii) Copy of the Enquiry Report, as Exhibit M-2. 


(iii) Copy of the letter dated 21/29.07.1998 issued by the Chief general Manager, Sodepur Area 
addressed to the Agent, BMP Group, regarding dismissal of Bhusan Agharia from service, as 
Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of the letter dated 03/05.08.1998 issued by the Agent, BMP Group, addressed to Bhusan 
Agharia informing him about his dismissal from service with immediate effect, as Exhibit M-4. 


(v) Copy of the Mercy Petition submitted on 04.07.2012 by Bhusan Agharia for his reinstatement, as 
Exhibit M-5. 


8. In cross-examination the management witness admitted that no second Show Cause Notice was issued to the 
workman before his dismissal. He further stated that management cannot produce any document to show that any 
communication was made to the workman in respect of his mercy petition. The witness also denied the suggestion that 
the company violated the principles of natural justice at the time of holding enquiry and that the dismissal was illegal. 


9. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative, advancing his argument on behalf of the dismissed workman 
submitted that initially a Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 was issued against the workman for unauthorized absence 
from duty for a period of four months and sixteen days only. The workman was suffering from illness during the 
period of absence and underwent medical treatment but on his reporting for duty he was not allowed to join. The 
management did not initiate any enquiry proceeding on the basis of the Charge Sheet. A second Charge Sheet was 
issued on 16.03.1998 alleging unauthorized absence of one year ninen months and twelve days but the same was not 
served upon the workman. No Notice of enquiry was served. The management in an arbitrary manner and in complete 
violation of principles of natural justice held enquiry in absence of the workman and dismissed him from service 
without complying the mandate of the Hon'ble Supreme Court of India by not issuing a second Show Cause Notice. It 
is argued that the order of dismissal was not communicated to the workman. After a long time when the workman 
came to know about his dismissal, he submitted mercy petitions for his reinstatement as per the Memorandum of 
Settlement dated 22.05.2007 but till date management has not considered the same though he fulfilled all requirement 
for his reinstatement. The union representative argued that the order of dismissal is disproportionate and illegal and 
the same is required to be set aside and the workman should be reinstated in his service with full back wages. 


10. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management in reply argued that the workman remained absent for a 
very long time but he neither participated in the enquiry proceeding nor did he file any document before this Tribunal 
in support of his claim that he underwent medical treatment at the time of his absence. Learned advocate argued that 
the dismissed workman had raised the Industrial Dispute after nineteen (19) years and the same is liable to be 
dismissed. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7537 


11. I have carefully considered the case and the counter case of the parties as disclosed in their written statements 
evidence adduced and rival argument advanced. It is undisputed that the workman was dismissed form service on 
05.08.1998 and the Industrial Dispute has been referred to this Tribunal in the year 2017 ie., after lapse of nineteen 
years. From the evidence led by the union, it appears that the mercy petitions were submitted by Bhusan Agharia for 
his reinstatement on 04.07.2012 (Exhibit W-3) and thereafter on 28.06.2013 (Exhibit W-4). Management of ECL did 
not consider the applications according to the terms of settlement in the Memorandum of Settlement dated 22.05.2007. 
In the case of Raghubir Singh Vs. General Manager, Haryana Roadways, Hissar [2014 (10) SCC 301], the 
Hon'ble Supreme Court of India emphasized the phrase “at any time” occurring in Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 would imply that law of limitation did not apply on the facts, and the court held that the State 
Government had rightly exercised its power and refer the dispute to the labour court within reasonable time, 
considering the circumstances in which the appellant therein was placed. It emerges from the settled law that there is 
no period of limitation for raising an Industrial Dispute under Section 10 of Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 
12. It is pertinent to consider whether Bhusan Agharia has been dismissed in a lawful manner. On a perusal of 
the order of dismissal dated 21/29.07.1998 passed by the Chief general Manager, Sodepur Area (Exhibit M-3), it 
appears that Bhusan Agharia, Underground Loader of Bejdih Colliery was dismissed from his service with immediate 
effect. The Disciplinary Authority while passing such vital order did not refer to the Charge Sheet and its date of 
issuance. The General Manager in the order of dismissal directed the Agent of BMP Group to communicate the same 
to Bhusan Agharia. The Agent of BMP Group in his letter dated 03/05.09.1998 (Exhibit M-4) communicated the order 
of dismissal to Bhusan Agharia and in that letter, he tried to bridge the gap by incorporating the particulars of the 
Charge Sheet, disclosing therein that it was with reference to the Charge Sheet bearing No. Bej/C-6/14/98/271 dated 
16.03.1998 issued to the workman. On perusal of the materials produced by the management it appears that the 
management witness in his affidavit-in-chief did not refer to the Charge Sheet dated 16.03.1998, on the basis of which 
the workman has been dismissed. The copy of the Charge Sheet which is the foundation of the enquiry proceeding has 
not been produced. Mr. Apurva Saurabh Singh (MW-1) in Paragraph No. 2 of his affidavit-in-chief stated that the ex- 
workman was absenting from duty since 04.04.1996 to 21.08.1996 i.e., 4 months and Charge Sheet dated 21.08.1996 
was issued to him for his unauthorized absence. In course of his examination-in-chief the management witness 
remained silent about the second Charge Sheet. During cross-examination management witness admitted that no 
second Show Cause Notice was issued to the workman. I find from the evidence on record that there is no clear 
statement by the management witness about service of Charge Sheet and Notice of enquiry upon the workman at any 
point of time. It becomes abundantly clear that under such circumstance, arising out of non-service of Notic of enquiry 
or Charge Sheet, the management held an ex-parte domestic enquiry without giving any opportunity to the workman 
to represent his case or to explain his reason for unauthorized absence. 
13. The Hon’ble Supreme Court of India in the case of Union of India and Others Vs. Mohd. Ramzan Khan 
[AIR (1991) SC 471], laid down the principle of law in following words : 

“ When the Inquiry Officer is not the Disciplinary Authority, the delinquent employee has a right to receive a 

copy of the inquiry officer’s report before the Disciplinary Authority arrives at its conclusion with regard to the 

charges levelled against him. A denial of the inquiry officer’s report before the Disciplinary Authority takes its 

decision on the charges, is denial of opportunity to the employee to prove his innocence and is a breach of 

principles of natural justice.” 
The principle laid down by the Hon’ble Supreme Court of India was enforced by the Coal India Limited by way of 
issuing a Circular bearing No. CIL C-5A(vi)/50774/28 dated 12.05.1994, wherein it has been clearly laid down that 
the charged employee had to be supplied with Enquiry Proceeding and Enquiry Report and a second Show Cause 
Notice had to be issued to him before taking any final decision of removing him from service. It is a well settled 
position of law that when the Disciplinary Authority and the Enquiry Officer are two different persons then the 
Disciplinary Authority must give an opportunity to the charged employee to submit his representation in respect of the 
findings of the Enquiry Officer and only thereafter arrived at a finding about the punishment that is to be imposed. In 
the instant case the management of ECL manifestly committed a chain of errors by issuing two separate Charge 
Sheets dated 21.08.1996 and 16.03.1998, not holding any enquiry in respect of the first Charge Sheet and thereafter 
acted in an arbitrary manner by holding ex-parte enquiry without service of Notice of enquiry. The Disciplinary 
Authority in his letter bearing No. Pd/C-6/98/36/1719 dated 21/29.07.1998 (Exhibit M-3) did not refer to the Charge 
Sheet on the basis of which Bhusan Agharia was being dismissed. It is palpably clear that no opportunity was given to 
the workman to defend his case at any point of time, not even at the stage where he is entitled to a second Show Cause 
Notice, which is mandatory. In my considered view dismissal of Bhusan Agharia from his service is a clear violation 
of the principles of natural justice and the dismissal of the workman is not legally tenable. In view of the fact and 
circumstances of the case I hold that the order of dismissal passed by the Chief General Manager, Sodepur Area dated 
21/29.07.1998 is not sustainable under law and the same is set aside. The workman having been illegally dismissed is 
entitled to be reinstated in his service. During evidence the workman failed to produce any document to prove that he 
was suffering from illness during the period of his long absence. No evidence has been adduced by the workman that 
during the period of his absence from duty under the E.C.L. he was not gainfully employed at any other place. 
Therefore, I hold that the workman is not entitled to any back wages for the period he was not in the employment of 
the company. 

Hence, 


7538 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest in favour of the workman. The order of dismissal of Bhusan 
Agharia bearing No. Pd/C-6/98/36/1719 dated 21/29.07.1998 passed by the Chief General Manager, Sodepur Area, 
Eastern Coalfields Limited is set aside. Management of Eastern Coalfields Limited is directed to reinstate Bhusan 
Agharia in the service of the company within one month from the date of communication of this Award maintaining 
continuity of his service. The workman is not entitled to any back wages during the period he was not in the 
employment of the company. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in 
duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERIEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 16 दिसम्बर, 2025 


का.आ. 2174.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 14) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल के पंचाट(संदर्भ संक्या-28/2007) को प्रकाशित 
करती है, जो केन्द्रीय सरकार को 15/12/2025 को प्राप्त हुआ था। 


[सं. एल-22012/40/2004-आईआर (सी.एम-1)] 


सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 


New Delhi, the 16th December, 2025 


S.O. 2174.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference.No./28/2007) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management 
of ECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 15/12/2025. 


[No. L-22012/40/2004 — IR (CM-ID)] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 


ANNEXURE

ASANSOL.

REFERENCE CASE NO. 28 OF 2007 


PARTIES: Joy Prakash Das 

Vs. 

Management of Shyamsundarpur Colliery, ECL 

REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Milan Kumar Bandyopadhyay, Advocate. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Goswami, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 23.10.2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/400/2004-IR(CM-II) dated 13.04.2007 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Shyamsundarpur Colliery under Bankola Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and 
their workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7539 


“ Whether the action of the management of Shyamsundarpur Colliery under Bankola Area of M/s. Eastern 
Coalfields Limited, PO: Ukhra, Distt. Burdwan in dismissing Shri Joy Prakash Das, Fitter Helper from 
service is legal and justified? If not, to what relief the workman is entitled? ” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22012/400/2004-IR(CM-II) dated 13.04.2007 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 03.05.2007 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2; Brief fact of the case as disclosed in the written statement of Joy Prakash Das filed on 18.06.2007 is that, 
Bara Lachhman Das was a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL) and 
posted at Tilaboni Colliery as Underground Loader. He was declared medically unfit for job in the Mines in the year 
1987 and according to the provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA) Joy 
Prakash Das was provided employment as a dependent of Bara Lachhman Das and he was posted at Kumardihi ‘B’ 
Colliery under Bankola Area of ECL as a piece rated Trammer. 


3: Subsequently, a complaint was lodged by one Bholanath Singh before the Chief Vigilance Officer of ECL 
alleging that Joy Prakash Das is not the son of Bara Lachhman Das and by impersonation he cheated the company for 
the purpose of securing employment for himself under ECL. On the basis of the complaint Personnel Manager, 
Bankola Area, ECL issued a letter dated 17.11.1987 addressed to the Officer-in-Charge, Charmondi Police Station, 
Bihar, requesting him to verify the identity of Joy Prakash Das, who is claiming to the son of Bara Lachhman Das. 
After holding an enquiry, the Officer-in-Charge of Charmondi Police Station informed the Personnel Manager that the 
workman Joy Prakash Das is the son of Bara Lachhman Das. In the meantime, the Agent Kumardihi Colliery issued a 
Charge Sheet bearing No. Agent/KD/PD/87/54/1275 dated 01.12.1987 against Joy Prakash Das alleging that he has 
produced false relationship certificate with Bara Lachhman Das, ex-employee, who is not his real father and by 
submitting false certificate he has procured an employment for himself which is a serious misconduct as per Section 
17(4)(0) of the Model Standing Orders which states as follows : 


“Giving false information regarding one’s name, age, father’s name, qualification or previous service at the time 
of the employment.” 


The workman replied to the charge levelled against him by denying the same as baseless, concocted and motivated. 
The Disciplinary Authority initiated a domestic enquiry against Joy Prakash Das in respect of such charge and after 
holding enquiry the charge could not be substantiated against him. 


4. On the basis of a First Information Report bearing R/C No. 80/88 - Calcutta dated 30.12.1988, a case was 
registered with the Central Bureau of Investigation (CBI), Special Police Establishment (SPE), Anti-Corruption 
Branch (ACB), Calcutta, under Section 120B/420/467/468/471/477A of the Indian Penal Code and Section 5(1)(d) 
read with 5(2) of the Prevention of Corruption Act, 1947, against Joy Prakash Das, Bara Lakshman Das, Lal Deo 
Singh and others of Bankola Area, ECL. After holding investigation CBI submitted a Final Report before the Court of 
Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate at Durgapur in Government Registered Case No. 219/89. Joy Prakash Das was 
discharged from the case but the Agent, Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery issued a second Charge Sheet bearing No. 
Agent/KB/PD/92/54/210 dated 29.01.1992 to the effect that Joy Prakash Das who is the son of Lal Deo Singh, Clerk, 
Grade-I at Bankola Area of ECL has falsely claimed himself to be the son of Bara Lachhman Das and cheated the 
management by giving false information regarding his name, age, father’s name at the time of obtaining the 
employment and thereby he is charged with the misconduct of theft, fraud or dishonesty in connection with the 
employer’s business or property and have aided and abetted the above misconduct and was directed to show cause 
within seventy-two hours of receipt of the Charge Sheet as to why disciplinary action would not be initiated. Joy 
Prakash Das filed a Writ Petition under Article 226 of the Constitution of India before the Hon'ble High Court at 
Calcutta, bearing Civil Order No. 1438(W) of 1992 where he challenged the validity of the second Charge Sheet dated 
29.01.1992. The writ petition was finally dismissed on 23.12.2002 and the domestic enquiry was started against the 
charged workman. Mr. B. N. Mishra, Personnel Manager, Moira Colliery was appointed as the Enquiry Officer. It is 
alleged that without holding a proper enquiry, without providing opportunity to the charged workman to be assisted by 
a co-worker and without adducing any evidence, the Enquiry Officer concluded the enquiry holding him guilty of the 
charge. It is alleged that the Enquiry Officer was biased as he is a subordinate officer of the Disciplinary Authority. 


5. Further contention of the workman is that the General Manager, Bankola Area without supplying copy of the 
Enquiry Proceeding and report and without issuing any second Show Cause Notice imposed a major punishment of 
dismissal against him, which is a violation of the mandatory provision. The workman after his dismissal, preferred a 
Writ Petition before the Hon'ble High Court at Calcutta bearing Writ Petition No. 1677 of 2003 contending that he has 
been dismissed in an illegal manner. The Hon'ble High Court disposed of the writ petition with an observation that the 
petitioner could seek efficacious alternative remedy under the provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. Hence, 
this Industrial Dispute has been raised before this Tribunal, challenging the enquiry proceeding and order of dismissal. 
The workman in his written statement has prayed for setting aside the order of dismissal and directing the 
management to reinstate him in his service with full back wages. 


7540 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


6. The management of ECL contested the Industrial Dispute by filing written statement on 10.08.2007. The 
specific case of the management is that Joy Prakash Das, claiming himself to be the son of Bara Lachhman Das 
obtained an employment on pre-matured termination of service of Bara Lachhman Das on medical ground and joined 
Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery of ECL. Management received information that he is not Joy Prakash Das, son of Bara 
Lachhman Das and on the contrary, he is Joy Prakash Singh, son of Lal Deo Singh, who also is also an employee of 
ECL posted at Shyamsundarpur Colliery. Management contended that the charged employee was a student of Andal 
Hindi Vidyalaya where his father’s name was recorded as Lal Deo Singh. The CBI registered a case bearing R/C No. 
80/88 over this matter under various penal provisions. The CBI after investigation submitted investigation report in 
final form before the Court of Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate at Durgapur with an observation that evidence 
collected against the workman is sufficient for initiating a departmental proceeding A second Charge Sheet dated 
29.01.1992 was issued and a proceeding was started against the workman where he was found guilty and thereafter 
the was dismissed from service by the Disciplinary Authority. It is asserted by the management that the dismissal of 
the workman from service is lawful and justified and the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


7. The crux of this Industrial Dispute is whether the dismissal of Joy Prakash Das, Fitter Helper from service of 
ECL is legal and justified, if not what relief the workman is entitled to. 


8. In support of his plea of innocence, the charged workman examined himself as Workman Witness No. 1. He 
has filed an affidavit-in-chief in the name of Joy Prakash Das as son of late Bara Lachhman Das. In his affidavit-in- 
chief he submitted his Voter Identity Card, PAN Card, old Ration Card, Relationship Certificate issued by the 
Pradhan, Ichapur Gram Panchayat. In his averment he stated that Bara Lachhman Das, his father was declared 
medically unfit for service in ECL and the company offered him employment against voluntary retirement of his 
father due to medical unfitness. Before providing employment management obtained Police Verification Report from 
his native village at Charmondi Police Station and on being satisfied by the report management offered him 
employment. It further transpires from his affidavit-in-chief that on the basis of a complaint lodged by one Bholanath 
Singh that Joy Prakash Das is not the son of Bara Lachhman Das, a Charge Sheet was issued and a domestic enquiry 
was held where he was found not guilty to the charge. The workman has reiterated his case disclosed in the written 
statement and produced the following documents on his re-examination on recall on 19.05.2023. 


(i) Copy of the Voter Identity card of Joy Prakash Das is produced as Exhibit W-1. 
(ii) Copy of the PAN Card of Joy Prakash Das, as Exhibit W-2. 
(iii) Copy of the Ration Card, as Exhibit W-3. 
(iv) Copy of the Identity Card issued by ECL, as Exhibit W-4. 
(v) Copy of the Appointment Letter of Joy Prakash Das, as Exhibit W-S. 
(vi) Copy of the complaint dated 18.09.1987 lodged by Bholanath Singh, as Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of letter dated 17.11.1987 issued by Personnel Manager, Bankola Area, to the Officer-in- 
Charge, Charmondi Police Station for verification of identity of Joy Prakash Das, as Exhibit W-7. 


(Vili) Copy of the first Charge Sheet dated 01.12.1987, as Exhibit W-8. 
(ix) Copy of the reply to the Charge Sheet dated 01.12.1987, as Exhibit W-9. 
(x) Copy of the Enquiry Proceeding dated 04.12.1987, as Exhibit W-10. 
(xi) Copy of the second Charge Sheet dated 29.01.1992, as Exhibit W-11. 


(xii) Copy of the letter dated 12/13.02.1992 whereby he was allowed to resume his duty pending enquiry 
on the basis of a Court’s Order, as Exhibit W-12. 


(xiii) Copy of the Application dated 08.02.2003 of Shyam Bihari Singh, a co-worker of Joy Prakash Das, 
submitted before the Enquiry Officer, seeking adjournment of the enquiry proceeding, as Exhibit W- 
13. 


(xiv) Copy of the letter dated 10.02.2003 issued by the Enquiry Officer addressed to Joy Prakash Das 
alias Joy Prakash Singh for adducing evidence and argument on 14.02.2003 is produced as Exhibit 
W-14. 


(xv) Copy of the Enquiry Proceeding, as Exhibit W-15. 
(xvi) Copy of the order dated 29.06.1992, passed by the Learned Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate, 
Durgapur, along with the Final Report of CBI, as Exhibit W-16. 


9. During cross-examination the witness stated that he joined his service on 11.07.1987 but did not deposit any 
certificate regarding his age. Witness denied the suggestion that he was a student of Andal Hindi Vidyalaya and 
further denied that Lal Deo Singh is his father. Workman witness denied the suggestion that he is actually Joy Prakash 
Singh and his father’s name is Lal Deo Singh, a Clerk at Bankola Area. 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7541 


10. Md. Abdul Rauf, Assistant Manager (Personnel), Shyamsundarpur Colliery is examined as Management 
Witness No. 1. He filed his affidavit-in-chief in support of the management’s case consistent with the written 
statement. The management witness claimed that dismissal of the workman is justified and he is not entitled to any 
relief. The management witness produced the following documents in support of his case : 


(i) Copy of the Enquiry Report in respect of Charge Sheet dated 29.01.1992 is produced as Exhibit M- 
1. 


(ii) Copy of the letter dated 10.02.2003 issued by the Enquiry Officer addressed to Joy Prakash Das 
alias Joy Prakash Singh for adducing evidence and argument on 14.02.2003 is produced as Exhibit 


M-2. 
(iii) Copy of the Dismissal letter dated 25/29.03.2003 issued by the General Manager, Bankola Area, as 
Exhibit M-3. 
11. In his cross-examination the management witness deposed that he has not produced any copy of complaint 


on the basis of which the identity of Joy Prakash Das was disputed. The witness failed to disclosed the name of the 
person who lodged the complaint. It is gathered from his testimony that in the year 1987 a Charge Sheet was issued 
against Joy Prakash Das for impersonation by him and a domestic enquiry was held where the charge levelled against 
the workman was held not proved. In the year 1992 another Charge Sheet was issued against Joy Prakash Das 
followed by a domestic enquiry. The witness was unable to state if Joy Prakash Das submitted any application dated 
08.02.2003 before the Enquiry Officer, disclosing his inability to appear in the enquiry proceeding due to his illness. 
The witness deposed that management did not issue any second Show Cause Notice before his dismissal. It further 
transpires that the copy of Enquiry Proceeding and Enquiry Report were not served upon the charged employee. 
However, witness denied that the workman was illegally and wrongly terminated from service and that he is not guilty 
of impersonation. 


12. Mr. Milan Kumar Bandyopadhyay, learned advocate, advancing his argument on behalf of Joy Prakash Das 
submitted that after appointment of Joy Prakash Das on the ground of medical unfitness of his father Bara Lachhman 
Das, a complaint was lodged before the Chief Vigilance Officer by one Bholanath Singh alleging that Joy Prakash 
Singh is the son of Lal Deo Singh. In the instant case the Enquiry Officer neither examined Bholanath Singh nor did 
the management representative produce the complaint on the basis of which the enquiry proceeding was started. 
Learned advocate argued that after receiving the alleged complaint from a fictitious person the Chief Vigilance 
Officer initiated an enquiry to find out the identity of the person but the report relating to the verification by police at 
Charmondi Police Station was also not produced before the Enquiry Officer. Referring to the Charge Sheet dated 
01.12.1987 (Exhibit W-8), learned advocate contended that the Enquiry Officer held an enquiry in respect of the 
charge that Joy Prakash Das for obtaining employment under ECL falsely claimed to be the son of Bara Lachhman 
Das and the Enquiry Officer found him not guilty of the charge. A CBI case was registered but on investigation into 
the matter, did not find any incremating materials against the accused workman resulting in submission of a Final 
Report before the Court of Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate at Durgapur and the Final Report was accepted without 
any further investigation. According to Mr. Bandyopadhyay a second Charge Sheet dated 29.01.1992 (Exhibit W-11) 
could not have been issued against the workman on the same allegation as it amounts to double jeopardy. Since the 
challenge against the second Charge Sheet was dismissed by the Hon'ble High Court at Calcutta dismissed Civil 
Order No. 1438(W) of 1992 challenging the second Charge Sheet, the domestic enquiry was conducted on the 
basis of second Charge Sheet and the charged employee without being given the opportunity of being represented by a 
co-worker and deprived of the opportunity of hearing was found guilty of charge in an illegal manner. Learned 
advocate further argued that a de novo enquiry was conducted by the second Enquiry Officer but the proceeding 
(Exhibit W-15) would clearly indicate that there is no material to prove the charge against the charged employee. 
Referring to the evidence of management witness, learned advocate heavily banked upon the fact that no second Show 
Cause Notice nor any enquiry proceeding or findings of the Enquiry Officer was served upon the workman before he 
was dismissed from service. Taking me through Enquiry Report, produced by the management witness as Exhibit M- 
1, Mr. Bandyopadhyay submitted that the report is not based upon the evidence but has simply referred to the findings 
of CBI in their investigation report without examining the witnesses. Therefore, such Enquiry Report is not tenable 
and the findings of the Enquiry Officer is grossly illegal, arbitrary and unfounded. It is urged that the order of 
dismissal is liable to be set aside and the workman should be reinstated in service along with consequential benefits 
like back wages from the date of his dismissal till his reinstatement. 


13. Mr. P. K. Goswami, learned advocate for the management argued that Joy Prakash Das taking recourse to 
impersonation and by falsely representing himself to be the son of Bara Lachhman Das has obtained employment at 
ECL at Kumardihi ‘B’ Colliery. Soon thereafter one Bholanath Singh complained that the name of the said person is 
not Joy Prakash Das but he is Joy Prakash Singh, the son of Lal Deo Singh. Initially, a Charge Sheet was issued 
against him on 01.12.1987 but he was exonerated from the charge. Thereafter, CBI investigated into the complaint in 
connection with R/C No. 80/88, giving rise to Government Registered Case No. 219/89 before the Court of Learned 
Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate, Durgapur. On completion of investigation CBI summitted a Final Report but 
observed that : 


7542 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


“ During investigation a certificate was seized which was issued under the signature of Sri J. P. Singh, Mukhia of 
Nuniatari Gram Panchyat. It was certified that Sri J. P. Das is the son of B. L. Das. During examination of Sri J. P. 
Singh denied his knowledge about the contents of the certificate but GEQD could not confirm this aspect. 


For want of sufficient and clinching evidences it will be very difficult to prove the criminal misconduct of the 
accused persons in the court of law. But evidences collected against Sri L. D. Singh (A-1) and Sri J. P. Das (A-2) 
during investigation were found sufficient for initiating departmental action as both the accused persons failed to 
maintain absolute integrity and devotion to duty and for this lapses the matter relating to them has been referred to 
the department (ECL authority) for taking departmental action against them. 


In view of the above, I submit Final Report as True u/s 173 Cr.P.C. against all the accused persons. 
It is therefore, prayed that the Final Report may kindly be accepted and necessary order may kindly be passed.” 


Learned advocate argued that CBI could not submit Charge Sheet against the accused person for want of clinching 
evidence to prove the misconduct but expressed the view that evidence collected against Lal Deo Singh and Joy 
Prakash Das, Accused No. 1 and 2 during investigation was sufficient to initiate Departmental Proceeding against 
them. Learned advocate submitted that on the basis of CBI’s report the management issued a second Charge Sheet 
dated 29.01.1992 (Exhibit W-11) and after giving full opportunity to the charged employee Joy Prakash Das alias Joy 
Prakash Singh, the Enquiry Officer found him guilty of the charge. The Disciplinary Authority after considering all 
the materials, issued a letter of dismissal bearing No. BA/PD/Dismissal/707 dated 25/29.03.2003 (Exhibit M-3). It is 
argued that the charge was established against the accused person that he is Joy Prakash Sigh and not Joy Prakash Das 
and the action of management of ECL is justified in dismissing the employee. 


14. I have considered the fact and circumstances of the case disclosed in the pleadings of the respective parties, 
evidence adduced, in the enquiry, Enquiry Proceeding, Findings of the Enquiry Officer Final Report of the CBI as 
well as the order of the Learned Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate, Durgapur passed in G.R. Case No. 219/89, 
accepting the Final Report of CBI and the rival arguments advanced by the parties. The principal charge against Joy 
Prakash Das is that he submitted false information regarding his name, relationship, age and other material particulars 
at the time seeking employment as the dependent son of Bara Lachhman Das, who was terminated from service on the 
ground of medical unfitness. It appears from the record that Bara Lachhman Das was terminated from his service on 
10.07.1987 and the charged employee was granted employment on 11.07.1987, on the very next date. On the basis of 
a complaint lodged before the Vigilance department of ECL an enquiry was conducted to verify the identity of Joy 
Prakash Das and a Charge Sheet was issued to Joy Prakash Das on 01.12.1987 (Exhibit W-8) to the effect that he 
produced false relationship certificate with Bara Lachhman Das, Underground Loader who is not his real father and 
got employment due to such serious misconduct a disciplinary action was proposed against him as per Section 17(i)(0) 
of the Model Standing Orders. In the first enquiry proceeding, the workman was exonerated from the charge. The CBI 
registered a case bearing R/C No. 80/88 under Section 120B/420/467/468/471/477A of the Indian Penal Code and 
Section 5(1)(d) read with 5(2) of the Prevention of Corruption Act, 1947. After holding an investigation the prime 
investigating agency submitted a Final Report on 25.06.1992 while observing that for want of sufficient and clinching 
evidence it will be very difficult to prove the criminal misconduct of the accused persons in the court of law and in the 
same breath in a conflicting manner the Investigating Officer observed that evidence collected against Lal Deo Singh 
and Joy Prakash Das during investigation were found sufficient to initiate a departmental action against the accused 
persons for the lapses. The legal position in criminal jurisprudence is whenever an investigating agency finds 
sufficient material against an accused person a duty is cast upon the agency to submit a Charge Sheet. The 
responsibility to consider whether there is clinching evidence against the accused person to prove the charge is vested 
not upon the investigating agency but with the adjudicating body i.e., the court of law. I find that CBI flawed in their 
investigation by not submitting a Charge Sheet even though it has opined that there is sufficient evidence to initiate 
departmental action against the persons. It is strange that no DNA Test or Paternity Test was held nor did the Enquiry 
Officer verify the relation from the school and relatives. Exhibit W-16 i.e., order dated 29.06.1992, passed by the 
Learned Sub-Divisional Judicial Magistrate, Durgapur discloses that when the Court directed the Investigating Officer 
to issue Notice upon the de facto complainant to appear before the Court on 28.09.1992 to ventilate their objection 
against the Final Report, learned Assistant Public Prosecutor attached to the Court submitted that no Notice need to be 
sent to the de facto complainant as the case was started by the CBI itself. It is unacceptable that on the basis of 
observation of the CBI to start a Departmental Proceeding a de novo Departmental Enquiry was initiated against the 
workman on the basis of the a fresh Charge Sheet issued on 29.1.1992 (Exhibit W-11) and not the first Charge Sheet 
dated 01.12.1987, disclosing the same charge. The Disciplinary Authority if not satisfied with the findings of the 
Enquiry Officer can direct further enquiry over the charge by a different Enquiry Officer and produced additional 
evidence to substantiate the charge but it is legally impermissible to hold a de novo enquiry against the same person. 
In the case of K. R. Deb Vs. Collector of Central Excise, Shillong [AIR 1971 SC 1447], the Hon'ble Supreme Court 
of India in a Special Leave Petition while considering whether multiple enquiries held against the appellant were in 
accordance with Rule 15 of the Classification and Control Rules observed : 


“ ,....... it may be possible if in a particular case there has been no proper inquiry because some serious defect has 


crept into the inquiry or some important witnesses were not available at the time of the inquiry or were not examined 


[भाग त---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7543 


for some other reason, the Disciplinary Authority may ask the Inquiry Officer to record further evidence. But there is 
no provision in R.15 for completely setting aside previous inquiries on the ground that the report of the Inquiring 
Officer or Officers does not appeal to the Disciplinary Authority. The Disciplinary Authority has enough powers to 
reconsider the evidence itself and come to its own conclusion under R.9. [379 H] 


The rules do not contemplate an action such as taken by the Collector in appointing a third Inquiry Officer. 
It seems that the Collector instead of taking responsibility himself was determined to get some officer to report against 
the appellant. The procedure adopted was not only against the rules but also harassing to the appellant. [380 B] In 
the result it must be held that no proper inquiry has been conducted in the case and, therefore, there has been a 
breach of Art. 311(2) of the Constitution. [380 E] ” 


Though the employer and employee of ECL are not governed by the Classification and Control Rules as in the 
aforesaid case, the principles of natural justice would apply and in my considered view a de novo second enquiry on 
the self-same matter appears to be a breach of legal principle amounting to a double jeopardy. 


15. The only scope and jurisdiction of their forum is to find out is the enquiry proceeding is legal and tenable. It 
is not for this Tribunal to find out if Joy Prakash Das is the son of Lal Deo Singh or Bara Lachhman Das. Independent 
of the above findings, if we look at the Enquiry Proceeding (Exhibit W-15) conducted by the second Enquiry Officer 
in respect of Charge Sheet dated 29.01.1992, it would appear that the charged employee participated in the enquiry 
and prayed for adjournment in absence of co-worker assisting him and also for recording his further statement. The 
Enquiry Officer did not grant adjournment to the charged employee for three days which appears to be a breach of the 
principles of natural justice and it appears that the Enquiry Officer concluded the enquiry proceeding hurriedly and 
arrived at this finding without following the principle of Audi alteram partem, that is giving opportunity of 
hearing to the charged employee according to the principles of natural justice. I further find that the Enquiry Officer 
himself compared the signature of Joy Prakash Das appearing in the Form ‘B’ Register and in the Service Record with 
the signature of Joy Prakash Singh appearing in the school admission form and observed that such signatures were of 
the same person. The Enquiry Officer considered the report of CBI and marked it as Ex-1. In the instant case without 
examining the witness, cited by the CBI in their investigation report and without ascertaining the true identity of Joy 
Prakash Das the findings of the Enquiry Officer that the charge levelled against Joy Prakash Das has been fully 
established is unsustainable. In order to substantiate such charge, the identity of such person has to be fully established 
to the effect that Joy Prakash Das and Joy Prakash Singh are one and the same person and prior to grant of 
employment no one existed as Joy Prakash Das, son of Bara Lachhman Das. Due to want of clinching evidence 
against the charged workman the report submitted by the second Enquiry Officer dated 19.02.2003 is not found 
sustainable and no order of dismissal could have been based upon such report. 


16. Admittedly, management has issued no second Show Cause Notice to the charged employee and copies of 
Enquiry Proceeding and Enquiry Report were not supplied to him for his response. According to the decision of the 
Hon'ble Supreme Court of India in the case of Union of India and Others Vs. Mohd. Ramzan Khan [AIR (1991) 
SC 471], laid down the principle of law in following words : 


“ When the Inquiry Officer is not the Disciplinary Authority, the delinquent employee has a right to receive a copy of 
the inquiry officer’s report before the Disciplinary Authority arrives at its conclusion with regard to the charges 
levelled against him. A denial of the inquiry officer’s report before the Disciplinary Authority takes its decision on the 
charges, is denial of opportunity to the employee to prove his innocence and is a breach of principles of natural 
justice.” 


The principle laid down by the Hon’ble Supreme Court of India was enforced by 


the Coal India Limited by way of issuing a Circular bearing No. CIL C-5A(vi)/50774/28 dated 12.05.1994, wherein it 
has been clearly laid down that the charged employee had to be supplied with Enquiry Proceeding and Enquiry Report 
and a 2nd Show Cause Notice had to be issued to him before taking any final decision of removing him from service. 
Therefore, I hold that the order of dismissal passed against the workman by the General Manager, Bankola Area, 
ECL, communicated through his letter dated 25/29.03.2003 is bad in law, arbitrary and the same is set aside. The 
workman be reinstated in his service within two months from the date of communication of the Award. On a scrutiny 
of the evidence on record, I find that the workman has not adduced any evidence that he was not in gainfully 
employed at any other place during the period he was out of employment of the company. Therefore, I am not inclined 
to grant any back wages to the workman for the period he rendered no service to the employer company. 
Hence, 
ORDERED 

that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest. The order of dismissal passed against the workman by the 
General Manager, Bankola Area, Eastern Coalfields Limited dated 25/29.03.2003 is set aside. The dismissed 
workman be reinstated in service of the company within 2 (two) months from the date of communication of the Award 
maintaining continuity of his service. The workman shall not be entitled to any back wages for the period he rendered 
no service to the company. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in 
duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


7544 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


नई दिल्‍ली, 16 दिसम्बर, 2025

का.आ. 2177.--औद्योगिक विवाद अधिनियम, 1947 (1947 का 144) की धारा 17 के अनुसरण में, केन्द्रीय 
सरकार ई सी एल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजकों और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में 
केन्द्रीय सरकार औद्योगिक अधिकरण-सह-श्रम न्यायालय, आसनसोल के पंचाट(एलसी/आर-22/2023) को प्रकाशित

[सं. एल-22012/34/2023-आईआर (सी.एम-1)] 
सलोनी, उप निदेशक/लिंक अधिकारी 


New Delhi, the 16th December, 2025 


9.0. 2177.—In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference. LC/R/22/2023) of the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal-cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management 
of ECL and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 15/12/2025. 

[No. L-22012/34/2023 - IR (CM-ID] 


SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer

ASANSOL.

Presiding Officer, 
C.G.I.T-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 22 OF 2023 


PARTIES: Jitendra Muchi and Sakunma Muchi 
(dependents of Late Binod Muchi) 
Vs. 
Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. 


For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 


[भाग त---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7547 


INDUSTRY: Coal 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 03.10.2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/34/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 26.04.2023 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Khas Kajora Colliery under Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“ Whether the action of the management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area, M/s. E.C.Ltd. in not 
providing compassionate appointment to Sri Jitendra Muchi, Son-in-law in place of his deceased father-in- 
law Late Binod Muchi, Ex-Dumper Khalasi and not paying monetary compensation to Smt. Sakunma Muchi 
wife of Late Biond Muchi are legal and justified. If not, what relief Sri Jitendra Muchi and Smt. Sakunma 
Muchi are entitled to? ” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22012/34/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 26.04.2023 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 02.05.2023 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. After receiving Notice Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on 12.06.2023 in support of the 
dependents of the deceased workman. The fact of the case as delineated in the written statement is that Binod Muchi 
was a permanent employee of Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL) and was posted as a 
Dumper Khalasi at Khas Kajora Colliery under Kajora Area of ECL. He died on 01.07.2010 while in service of ECL, 
leaving behind his wife Sakunma Muchi, four daughters, namely Baby Muchi, Sarita Kumari, Kabita Kumari and Arti 
Kumari, two sons namely Raj Kumar Muchi and Kabi Kumar Muchi. At the time of death, the sons were minor and 
Baby Muchi, the eldest daughter was married to Jitendra Muchi who was dependent upon Binod Muchi. Sakunma 
Muchi, the widow applied for providing employment to her in place of her husband as per provisions of National Coal 
Wage Agreement (hereinafter referred to as NCWA). Due to her physical condition, she was found unfit for 
employment and was not provided any employment. The sons were minor and the only eligible dependent who was 
nominated for employment was Jitendra Muchi, the son-in-law who was residing with Binod Muchi. Sakunma Muchi, 
filed a separate application for providing employment to Jitendra Muchi, the dependent son-in-law of Binod Muchi 
and also claimed Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation (hereinafter referred to as MMCC) for herself. After 
screening at the Colliery and Area level the son-in-law was asked to appear before Initial Medical Examination Board 
(hereinafter referred to as IME Board) for his test. After examination by IME Board, Jitendra Muchi was found 
medically fit for employment and the proposal was sent to ECL Headquarters with recommendation of the General 
Manager of Kajora Area. ECL Headquarters made some queries regarding Jitendra Muchi which were replied by the 
dependent son-in-law but till date ECL Headquarters has not approved the proposal for employment of the son-in-law. 
The grievance of the dependents of the deceased employee is that the management has not acted in accordance with 
the provisions of NCWA and deprived the family of the deceased employee from getting benefits of employment and 
monetary compensation. Further case of the union is that the marriage between Jitendra Muchi and Baby Muchi, 
daughter of the deceased employee was solemnized in the year 2009 and the son-in-law was residing with Binod 
Muchi as he was suffering from illness and there was no other male member to look after him. It is contended that 
Jitendra Muchi has no source of income to maintain himself and the family of Binod Muchi. The union has prayed 
that the son-in-law should be provided with employment according to NCWA and the widow should be paid monetary 
compensation from the date of death of the employee i.e., 01.07.2010 till the employment is provided to the son-in- 
law. 


3 Management contested the case by filing written statement on 19.07.2023. Management admitted the fact 
that Binod Muchi was a permanent employee of ECL and posted at Khas Kajora Colliery bearing U.M. No. 249880 
who expired on 01.07.2010. The counter case of the management is that Sakunma Muchi, wife of Binod Muchi had 
applied for employment after the death of her husband. Subsequently, she was referred to IME on 24.02.2011 where 
she was declared unfit for job. Thereafter, Jitendra Muchi, claiming to be the son-in-law of Binod Muchi applied for 
employment against the death of Binod Muchi but the claim cannot be processed due to want of document from the 
claimant. Management asserted that claim of an indirect dependent i.e., son-in-law can be considered only when there 
no other direct dependent family member of the deceased employee is available. In the present case there are direct 
family members of the deceased employee, therefore, there is no provision for providing employment to the son-in- 
law as per guidelines of the company. It is urged that the dispute for employment was raised before the Assistant 
Labour Commissioner (C) after laps of twelve years in the year 2022 from the date of death of Binod Muchi and then 


7548 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


referred to this Tribunal in the year 2023 i.e., after more than thirteen years from the date of death of the employee. 
Hence, the dispute is stale. Management in the premises stated above urged that het dependent son-in-law is not 
entitled to any employment and the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


4. Union examined Jitendra Muchi as Workman Witness No. 1. He has filed his affidavit-in-chief claiming that 
Binod Muchi, his father-in-law died on 01.07.2010 at Central Hospital, Kalla, ECL, leaving behind his wife, 
daughters, sons and son-in-law. At the time of death of Binod Muchi, he left behind no direct dependent for claiming 
employment. All the dependents were minor, as such his mother-in-law initially submitted application for 
employment for herself but she was found medically unfit for employment. Thereafter, Sakunma Muchi submitted 
application for employment of her son-in-law. No objection was submitted by the other dependents of Binod Muchi in 
respect of claim for employment of Jitendra Muchi and the management held his medical examination through IME 
Board. The proposal for his employment was forwarded to ECL Headquarters but the same is pending without any 
consideration at ECL Headquarters at Sanctoria and no decision has been taken by the management nor any letter has 
been issued regretting his claim for employment. In course of evidence-in-chief the workman witness has produced 
the following documents : 


(i) Copy of the Death Certificate of Binod Muchi issued by the Central Hospital, Kalla has been 
produced as Exhibit W-1. 


(ii) Copy of the Death Registration Certificate of Binod Muchi, as Exhibit W-2. 
(iii) Copy of the Service Book of Binod Muchi, as Exhibit W-3. 
(iv) Copy of the letter dated 12.02.2013 for IME of Jitendra Muchi, as Exhibit W-4. 


(v) Copy of the Notice dated 08.09.2015 for Screening of Jitendra Muchi for employment, as Exhibit 
W-5. 


(vi) Copy of another Notice dated 05.10.2015 for Screening of Jitendra Muchi for employment, as 
Exhibit W-6. 


(vii) Copy of the Marriage Deed of Jitendra Muchi and Baby Muchi, as Exhibit W-7. 


(Vili) Copy of the application dated 10.07.2019 of Jitendra Muchi to the Director (Personnel), ECL, 
Sanctoria, requesting early action for providing employment, as Exhibit W-8. 


(ix) Copy of the Voter Identity Card of Jitendra Muchi, as Exhibit W-9. 
(x) Copy of the Wedding Invitation Card of Jitendra Muchi and Baby Muchi, as Exhibit W-10. 


5. From the cross-examination of the workman witness it transpires that the witness was twenty-three years old 
at the time of applying for employment. The witness further stated that he does not have any Ration Card issued from 
the local area of his father-in-law. The witness also stated that he had cast his vote at Khas Kajora Colliery for the first 
time in the year 2014 and that his name has been registered in the voters’ list of both at Jamui and Khas Kajora 
Colliery. The witness denied the suggesting that he is not a dependent of Binod Muchi and at the time of marriage his 
marriage, address of Baby Muchi was at Jamui and she used to reside at Jamui, Bihar at the relevant time. 


6. Sakunma Muchi, the widow was examined as Workman Witness No. 2. In her evidence-in-chief she deposed 
that she prayed for employment of her son-in-law as her sons are minor at the relevant time and the management also 
called him for appearing before the Screening Committee and IME Board. Jitendra Muchi was found fit for 
employment and his proposal for employment was sent to the ECL Headquarters. 


7. In cross-examination the witness deposed that Chauman Muchi (Ravidas) is the father of Jitendra Muchi and 
he used to reside with his father but after the marriage he started residing with them i.e., with the family of Binod 
Muchi. The witness produced Voter Identity Card of Jitendra Muchi as Exhibit W-9 and deposed that in the Voter 
Identity Card his address has been recorded as KhasKajora Colliery. Witness denied the suggestion that after her 
daughter’s marriage Jitendra Muchi was not residing with them or he bore no responsibility to look after them. The 
witness further stated that Raj Kumar Muchi and Kabi Kumar Muchi are two sons aged eight years and five years 
respectively at the time of her husband’s death. She also stated that she did not file any application for monetary 
compensation after the death of her husband. 


8. Mr. Proloy Dasgupta, Manager (Personnel), Khas Kajora Colliery has been examined as Management 
Witness No. 1. From his affidavit-in-chief it is gathered that Jitendra Muchi claimed employment as son-in-law of 
Binod Muchi and he applied for his employment on 26.12.2011 against the death of Binod Muchi. The prayer for 
employment of Jitendra Muchi was not processed for want of document from the claimant. The witness further stated 
in the affidavit-in-chief that according to the rules of the company the claim for employment by an indirect dependent 
could be considered only when no direct dependent of the deceased employee is available. In this case there are 
several direct dependents of the deceased employee, as such there is no scope for providing employment to the son-in- 
law as per guidelines of the company. The witness further stated that the dispute for employment has been raised 
before the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C) in the year 2022 i.e., after twelve years from the date of death of the 
employee and thereafter referred to this Tribunal in the year 2023 i.e., thirteen years after death of the ex-employee. In 


[भाग त---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7549 


course of his evidence management witness produced the following documents : 
(i) Copy of the Service Book of Binod Muchi has been produced as Exhibit M-1. 
(ii) Copy of the Death Certificate of Binod Muchi issued by the Central Hospital, Kalla, as Exhibit M-2. 
(iii) Copy of the Death Registration Certificate of Binod Muchi, as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of the application of Sakunma Muchi dated 16.08.2010, claiming employment against death of 
her husband, as Exhibit M-4. 


(v) Copy of the pre-employment medical examination report in Form-III, as Exhibit M-S. 


(vi) Copy of the application of Jitendra Muchi dated 26.12.2011, claiming employment against death of 
his father-in-law, as Exhibit M-6. 


From the evidence of Management Witness No. 1, I find that the management witness has clearly admitted that 
management of the company did not respond to the application of son-in-law and no communication was made citing 
reason for non-accepting his prayer. 


9. In course of cross-examination the management witness deposed that Jitendra Muchi was referred for his 
screening and IME, the copy of the application dated 24.08.2012 asking Jitendra Muchi to appear before IME has 
been Exhibit as M-7. Witness disclosed that no proposal for employment of the son-in-law was sent to the ECL 
Headquarters and they did not issue any letter of regret. Witness also stated that at the time of screening of Sakunma 
Muchi, the Screening Committee did not make any finding regarding eligibility of any son or daughter of the deceased 
employee for getting employment under ECL. He further stated that management did not verify if Jitendra Muchi is 
the son-in-law of Binod Muchi and as per the record no monetary compensation was provided to the widow of the 
deceased employee. 


10. The Industrial Dispute which arose for consideration is whether management of Khas Kajora Colliery is 
justified in not providing compassionate appointment to Jitendra Muchi, the son-in-law and non-payment of monetary 
compensation to Sakunma Muchi, the wife of the deceased employee is legal and justified. 


11. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative, arguing the case for the dependents of Binod Muchi submitted that 
after death of Binod Muchi on 01.07.2010, Sakunma Muchi filed an application dated 16.08.2010 for her employment 
(Exhibit M-4) but she was found medically unfit on the basis of medical examination held on 24.12.2011 (Exhibit M- 
5). Jitendra Muchi, dependent son-in-law prayed for employment by submitting application 26.12.2011 (Exhibit M-6). 
The management on finding Jitendra Muchi eligible for employment as a dependent as per Clause 9.3.2 of NCWA 
summoned him to appear before the Screening Committee on 24.08.2012 in the Office of the Personnel Department of 
Khas Kajora Colliery. Having fulfilled the basic requirements, Jitendra Muchi was directed to appear before the IME 
Board on 22.02.2013 on the basis of a letter dated 12.02.2013 (Exhibit W-4). Jitendra Muchi was once again asked to 
appear before the Screening Committee with relevant documents like, Service Record Excerpt, Death Certificate, 
Death Registration Certificate, PS-3 and PS-4 Forms, SC/ST Certificate, Voter Identity Card, Educational Certificate, 
etc. along with family members on 17.09.2015 (Exhibit W-5). Notice bearing No. KA/PM/C-6/2015/702 dated 
05.10.2015 (Exhibit W-6) was issued for the purpose of screening of Jitendra Muchi as dependent / nominee of Late 
Binod Muchi for employment and was again advised to appear before the Screening Committee with relevant 
documents on 16.10.2015. It is argued that Jitendra Muchi produced his Marriage Registration Deed (Exhibit W-7) 
and Wedding Invitation Card (Exhibit W-10) but the management did not inform him about the final decision 
regarding employment. The union representative strenuously argued that this is a fit case where the son-in-law was 
medically found fit and should be provided employment as dependent according to the provisions of NCWA and the 
wife of the deceased employee should be paid monetary compensation until employment is provided to the dependent 
son-in-law. 


12. In reply, Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that since there are other direct 
dependents of the deceased employee at the time of his death the son-in-law is not eligible for employment. It is 
further argued that the son-in-law is residing with his paternal family at Jamui and he was not dependent on his father- 
in-law at the time of death as such he is not entitled to employment. Learned advocate argued that Sakunma Muchi did 
not claim any monetary compensation, therefore, she is not entitled to any monetary compensation only on the basis 
of the Industrial Dispute raised. 


13. I have considered the arguments advanced by the union representative and learned advocate for the 
management in the light of fact and circumstances of the case emerging from their respective pleadings as well as 
evidence adduced by the parties. The facts that Binod Muchi was an employee of ECL at Khas Kajora Colliery and he 
died in harness on 01.07.2010 are admitted by the management. It is also established by evidence on record that 
Jitendra Muchi is the son-in-law of Binod Muchi and he married Baby Muchi on 29.04.2009. After death of Binod 
Muchi, the wife of the deceased employee claimed employment as a dependent but from Exhibit M-5 it is gathered 
that she was found unfit for job on the basis of medical examination dated 24.12.2011. According to the provision of 
Clause 9.3.1 of National Coal Wage Agreement employment could be provided to one dependent of the workman who 


7550 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


is disabled permanently and also who dies while in the service. It is further laid down in Clause No. 9.3.3 of NCWA 
that : 


“the dependant for this purpose means the wife/husband as the case may be, unmarried daughter, son and 
legally adopted son. If no such direct dependant is available for employment, brother, widowed 
daughter/widowed daughter-in-law or son-in-law residing with the deceased and almost wholly dependent on 
the earnings of the deceased may be considered to be the dependant of the deceased.” 


In the present case it appears that at the time of death of Binod Muchi, beside Sakunma Muchi, wife, who was found 
unfit for employment there were three unmarried daughters namely Sarita Kumari, Kabita Kumari and Arti Kumari 
who were 17 (seventeen) years, 12 (twelve) years and 10 (ten) years of age respectively and two sons namely, Raj 
Kumar Muchi and Kabir Muchi who were 8 (eight) years and 5 (five) years of age respectively at the relevant time. 


14. It is well settled position of law that terms for compassionate employment applicable to ECL and its 
employees are governed by NCWA, which is a negotiated agreement reflecting a collective decision of employer and 
employees and has the force of settlement as per Section 2(p) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 and is binding upon 
the parties. The terms of NCWA therefore have to be strictly construed while deciding the claims for employment and 
monetary compensation. A dependent son-in-law residing with the family of the deceased would be considered 
eligible for employment only if no ‘direct dependent’ is available in the family of the deceased. In the present case 
Binod Muchi has left behind no less than 5 (five) direct dependents, out of them names of at least two unmarried 
daughters could have been considered for employment after they attained majority. In such view of the matter, I hold 
that Jitendra Muchi, the son-in-law is not entitled to compassionate employment against the death of Binod Muchi, his 
father-in-law in existence of direct dependents. The management of Khas Kajora Colliery shall consider any eligible 
unmarried daughter nominated by Sakunma Muchi for employment on her attaining majority. In the meantime, 
management shall pay MMCC to Sakunma Muchi as per provision under Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA from the date of 
death of Binod Muchi till any of the dependent son or unmarried daughter is provided with employment or she reaches 
60 (sixty) years of age whichever is earlier. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is allowed in part in favour of dependents of Binod Muchi. Jitendra Muchi, the 
son-in-law is not entitled to any compassionate employment in existence of any other direct dependents. Management 
of Eastern Coalfields Limited is directed to consider the name of any nominated direct dependent of Late Binod 
Muchi for providing employment on attaining majority. Sakunma Muchi, the widow shall be paid Monthly Monetary 
Cash Compensation at the prevailing rate as per provision under Clause 9.5.0 (iii) of National Coal Wage Agreement 
from the date of death of Binod Muchi till employment is provide to any of the dependent or till she attains 60 (sixty) 
years, whichever is earlier. Let an award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the Award in 
duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERIEE, Presiding Officer

REFERENCE CASE NO. 14 OF 2023 
PARTIES: Chando Majhi and Budhani Mejhain 
(dependents of Late Mangru Majhi) 
Vs. 
Management of Jambad OCP, ECL
