Dated: 08.10.2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (1) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-22012/12/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 07.02.2023 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Jambad OCP under Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the Management of Jambad OCP, Kajora Area, M/s E.C.Ltd. in not providing 
compassionate appointment to Sri Chando Majhi in place of his deceased father Late Mangru Majhi, Ex- 
E.P.Helper (U.M.NO.578049) and not providing Monthly Monetary Compensation (MMS) to Smt. Budhani 
Mejahin wife of Late Mangru Majhi is fair, legal and justified? If not, what relief the dependent of the 
deceased workman concerned is entitled to and what directions are necessary in this respect? ” 


1 On receiving Order No. L-22012/12/2023-IR(CM-II) dated 07.02.2023 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case was registered on 14.02.2023 and an 
order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear and submit their 
written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Dependents of Late Mangru Majhi filed written statement on 04.08.2023. Fact of the case in brief as per 
the written statement is that Mangru Majhi was posted as E.P. Helper at Jambad OCP under Kajora Area of Eastern 
Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL) and died in harness on 01.05.2001, leaving behind Budhani 
Mejhain as widow and Chando Majhi as minor son. On 18.06.2001 Budhani Mejhain submitted an application 
before the management claiming employment for herself as per provisions of National Coal Wage Agreement 
(hereinafter referred to as NCWA). The management of ECL did not process her claim for employment and asked 
her to express her option to receive Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation (hereinafter referred to as MMCC) in 
lieu of employment. In course of time management did not pay monetary compensation to Budhani Mejhain nor did 
they communicate anything to her for four long years. After such delay on the part of the management the widow of 
the deceased submitted an application on 14.11.2005 praying for employment for her son Chando Majhi. The 
management did not respond to the prayer for employment of the dependent for which a complaint was made in the 
grievance portal. The dependents of the deceased were then called for screening over a period of time in the year 
2013, 2015, 2016, 2017 and 2019. Management called for some documents for processing employment of Chando 
Majhi which was compiled by the dependents within time. Budhani Mejhain also attended screening on 05.09.2019 
for payment of MMCC-. In a later course of time the prayer for employment of Chando Majhi was processed and sent 
to ECL Headquarters bearing file No. ED/4665 but the claim was rejected on 10.12.2020 on the pretext that it was 
not possible to reopen the claim file after twenty years of death of ex-employee. The dependent son of the deceased 
contended that correspondence was made claiming benefit till 2019 therefore the ground for rejection for prayer for 


(i) 
(ii) 


(iii) 


(iv) 


(v) 


(vi) 


7552 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


employment was bad in law and amounted to breach of rules of the company. It is urged that the management has 
recached a wrong decision by refusing employment to the dependent in contravention to the provisions of NCWA. 
Chando Majhi has prayed for his employment as a dependent. 


3. Management of ECL contested the case by filing written statement on the same date i.e., 04.08.2023. 
According to the management Budhani Mejhain applied for providing her employment on 24.09.2001 without any 
supporting document. After four years she submitted an application before the Agent of Jambad OCP for providing 
employment to her son without any document. Chando Majhi did not submit any application for his own 
employment. On 18.10.2014, after a lapse of nine years Budhani Mejhain requested the management for 
employment of her son for the second time and submitted documents. At that time Chando Majhi also submitted a 
separate application for employment thirteen years after the death of his father. The reason for delay in submitting 
the application as stated by Budhani Mejhain is that her son was a minor at that time. Management contended that 
the claim for employment of Chando Majhi was rejected by Kajora Area authority as per letter No. KA/PM/C- 
6/35/2017/545 dated 31.01.2017 on the ground that it was a belated case and as per company’s circular the case 
cannot be considered. Management of ECL in their written statement also placed reliance upon the decision of the 
Hon'ble Supreme Court of India in the case of (i) M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd Vs. Anil Badyakar and Others 
[Civil Appeal No. 3597 of 2009], wherein it was held that compassionate appointment is not vested right which can 
be exercised at any time in future. The compassionate employment cannot be claimed and offered after a lapse of 
time and after the crisis is over. Further reference has been made to the decision of the Hon’ble Division Bench of 
the Hon'ble High Court at Calcutta in the case of (ii) State of West Bengal & Others Vs. Purnam Pradhan 
[M.A.T. No. 1036 of 2011], wherein it was observed that compassionate appointment should not be offered after 
lapse of reasonable period, to say five years. Reliance is also placed upon the decision of the Hon'ble High Court at 
Calcutta in the case of (tii) Nunibala Mondal @ Nuni Mondal and Others Vs. Eastern Coalfields Limited and 
Others [W.P. 1660 (W) of 2016], wherein it was observed that it is judicially recognized that a delay of more than 
five or six years is fatal to a matter pertaining to compassionate appointment. 


4. According to the management Budhani Mejhain submitted an application dated 22.08.2019 for payment of 
MMCC. The screening was done at Unit Level and proposal was sent to Kajora Area Office along with required 
documents on 12.09.2019. However, the proposal for MMCC to Budhani Mejhain was not considered by the 
Competent Authority on the ground of delay and communication was made to her vide letter No. KAJ/Pers./C- 
6/35/2020/1283 dated 23.12.2020 informing that she could not make her choice for a long period and misguided the 
management by submitting contradictory application regarding employment and compensation. As per record the 
prayer for employment of Chando Majhi was regretted in 2017 since his claim was a belated one and the prayer for 
monetary compensation to Budhani Mejhain could not be considered as the application was made nineteen years 
after the cause of action. Management accordingly prayed for dismissal of the Industrial Dispute. 


५ The short point for consideration in this case is whether the action of management of Jambad OCP under 
Kajora Area of ECL is justified in not providing compassionate appointment to Chando Majhi and MMCC to 
Budhani Mejhain, dependents of the deceased employee, Mangru Majhi. If not, what relief the dependents of the 
deceased are entitled to. 


6. Union examined Chando Majhi as Workman Witness No. 1. He filed an affidavit-in-chief in support of the 
claim made on behalf of the dependents. It is stated that Mangru Majhi, the father of Chando Majhi was an employee 
of ECL at Jambad OCP. He was posted as an E.P. Helper and expired on 01.05.2001. Budhani Mejhain, the wife of 
the deceased employee at first applied for her employment on 18.06.2001 in place of her husband. The management 
asked her to opt for MMCC instead of employment and his mother agreed to take MMCC. The management did not 
sanction any monetary compensation in favour of the widow of the deceased employment. After waiting for four 
long years, Budhani Mejhain submitted an application on 14.11.2005, claiming employment for her son Chando 
Majhi. Management called the widow for her screening for the purpose of monetary compensation on 05.09.2019. 
The management thereafter denied monetary compensation to the dependent wife and no employment was provided 
to the dependent son. In course of evidence the workman witness produced the following documents :

7. In course of cross-examination the witness deposed that his date of birth is 01.04.1986 and denied the 
suggestion that management of the company has rightly refused his prayer for employment as a dependent of his 
deceased father. The witness could not state if his mother submitted any application before the company for 
maintaining his name in the Live Roster of the company for employment. At the time of hearing of the witness, he 
was engaged as a day labour in masonry work. It also transpires from the evidence that his mother is alive. 


8. Management of ECL examined Mr. Ashish Mohan, Deputy Manager (Personnel), Jambad OCP as 
Management Witness No. 1. The witness filed an affidavit-in-chief, wherein it is admitted that Mangru Majhi, an ex- 
employee of Jambad OCP expired on 01.05.2001. Budhani Mejhain, the wife of the deceased applied for her 
employment on 24.09.2001 without submitting any document. After four years from the date of death of her husband 
an application dated 22.08.2005 was submitted before the Agent, Jambad Colliery, claiming employment for her 
son. No separate application was submitted by Chando Majhi, son of the deceased employee for his employment. 
Management further stated that on 18.10.2014 after lapse of nine years, Budhani Mejhain requested the management 
for employment of her son Chando Majhi and submitted documents. According to the management witness, Budhani 
Mejhain disclosed that she submitted application for employment of her son after a delay as the son was a minor at 
the time of death of her husband, and the application was submitted after Chando Majhi became a major. 
Management witness further stated in the affidavit-in-chief that Budhani Mejhain submitted an application on 
22.08.2019 claiming for her MMCC and after screening was held at Unit Level a proposal was sent to Kajora Area 
Office along with all required documents on 12.09.2019 but the proposal was not considered by the Competent 
Authority on the ground of delay and the same was communicated through letter bearing No. KAJ/Pers./C- 
6/35/2020/1283 dated 23.12.2020. The claim for employment of Chando Majhi, the dependent son was regretted in 
the year 2017 due to submission of belated claim. The Competent Authority also declined the prayer for monetary 
compensation to Budhani Mejhain due to long delay of nineteen years. Management has produced a copy of letter 
dated 23.12.2020 issued by the Assistant Personnel Manager, Kajora Area addressed to the Agent, Jambad OCP, 
rejecting the prayer for MMCC and a copy of letter dated 21.01.2021 issued by the Assistant Manager (Personnel), 
Jambad OCP addressed to Budhani Mejhain, communicating the same. Management has produced the following 
documents in support of their converse case :

(ix) Copy of the letter dated 21.01.2021 issued by the Assistant Manager (P), Jambad OCP addressed to 
Budhani Mejhain, informing that her claim for MMCC has been rejected, as Exhibit M-9. 


9. In course of cross-examination the management witness (M.W.- 1) deposed that the application submitted 
by Budhani Mejhain on 18.06.2001 was not processed due to want of some necessary documents. Management did 
not inform Budhani Mejhain that some documents were necessary for processing her prayer. It transpires from his 
cross-examination that the management issued a letter dated 23/26.09.2019 (Exhibit W-3), requesting Budhani 
Mejhain to submit documents for processing her proposal for MMCC against the death of her husband. It further 
appears from his evidence that two years after issuing the Notice for screening, the prayer for payment of 
MMCC was regretted. After appearing before the Screening Committee and producing necessary documents the 
application of Chando Majhi for employment is rejected on the ground of delay in making the claim. 


10. Mr. Milan Kumar Bandyopadhyay, learned advocate for the union argued that on the death of Mangru 
Majhi on 01.05.2001, the dependent wife submitted an application for employment on 18.06.2001 i.e., within two 
months from the date of death but the management did not process her prayer for employment nor did they ask her to 
produce any document. Referring to Exhibit W-3, learned advocate argued that only on 26.06.2019 i.e., after lapse of 
eighteen years, management requested the widow of the deceased employee to appear before the Screening 
Committee for her MMCC and after spending some more time issued a letter dated 21.01.2021 (Exhibit W-4) 
expressing inability of the Competent Authority to consider the claim for MMCC on the pretext that nineteen long 
years have passed after the date of death of ex-employee. Learned advocate strenuously argued that the management 
being fully aware about the date of death of the employee in harness, the rights of the dependent and well settled fact 
that the claimant is the widow of the employee as per the Service Record Excerpt (Exhibit W-11) did not take 
effective step to meet the claim of the dependent as per provisions of Clause 9.5.0 of NCWA. It is argued that the 
management of ECL has committed inordinate delay in processing the claim of the dependents for eighteen years 
and now to cover up their own fault regretted the claim for monetary compensation. It is contended that such act on 
the part of management is arbitrary and the management of ECL should be directed to pay the monetary 
compensation to the widow of the deceased from the date of death till her son is provided with employment. Learned 
advocate further argued that Chando Majhi, the son, was a minor at the relevant time and his name appears in the 
Service Record Excerpt of the deceased employee (Exhibit W-11). As per the Service Record Excerpt, Chando 
Majhi was two years old on 01.04.1987 and his age can be calculated as fifteen (15) years at the time of death of his 
father. The widow had promptly submitted her application for employment on 18.06.2001 (Exhibit W-2), waited for 
considerable period and when management did not respond and extend any cooperation to process the employment, 
the widow submitted an application on 22.08.2005 claiming for employment of her son (Exhibit W-5). It is argued 
that the management of ECL was silent over this issue until 2015 and it was on 08.02.2015 the Agent, Jambad OCP 
issued a letter to Chando Majhi for appearing before the Screening Committee on 18.02.2015 for his employment 
(Exhibit W-6). The management thereafter issued letter dated 13.08.2015 for screening of Chando Majhi on 
17.08.2015 (Exhibit W-7) and a letter dated 09.06.2016 for holding screening on 14.06.2016 (Exhibit W-8). For the 
last time management issued a letter on 16.01.2017 addressed to Chando Majhi for holding screening on 17.01.2017 
for his employment (Exhibit W-9). Learned advocate for the union argued that the management took twelve long 
years for holding screening of the dependent son for his employment only to communicate him by letter dated 
11.02.2017 that his prayer for employment was rejected as his case could not be considered for belated submission 
of application as per the company’s circular. Learned advocate for the union argued that the management has acted 
in an arbitrary and illegal manner as there is no time limit in NCWA for raising claim for employment. Furthermore, 
the management cannot derive advantage of their own wrong by delaying the entire process for several years and 
thereafter by harping upon their own fault of delay, reject the prayer for employment of the dependent. 


11. Mr. Milan Kumar Bandyopadhyay, learned advocate for the union referred to a Memorandum of Settlement 
under Section 12(3) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, arrived at between the management of ECL and their 
workmen, represented by AITUC, BMS, CITU, HMS, INTUC, and UTUC before the Regional Labour 
Commissioner (C), Asansol on 22.05.2007, wherein in Point No. 2 the management had agreed to clear all the 
employment cases prior to 01.07.1996 without any time bar. For cases received after 01.07.1996, the existing time 
limit of one year would be continued and management consented that they would approach the ECL Board in the 
next Board Meeting for waiver of time limit for consideration of employment proposal to the dependent as no time 
bar existed in the NCWA. Learned advocate summing up his argument urged that in the instant case the dependent 
son is entitled to employment and he cannot be disqualified for management’s own fault and the widow of the 
deceased employee is also entitled to MMCC according to the provisions of NCWA which strictly binds both the 
employer and employee. 


12. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that time is an essential ingredient for 
acts performed by the parties. The dependent of the workman cannot be allowed to submit their application claiming 
benefits at any point of time. It is argued that complete application for employment of the son was submitted on 
18.10.2014 by the mother and the son (Exhibit M-3 and M-4). It is further contended that Budhani Mejhain in the 
letter dated 05.07.2016 (Exhibit M-5) has stated that at first, she applied for employment in place of her deceased 
husband but on the advice of the Competent Authority she claimed for monetary compensation instead of 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7555 


employment as her son was minor at that time. Learned advocate for the management stated that the cause of delay 
in submitting the application has also been mentioned in the application of Budhani Mejhain dated 05.07.2016. It is 
submitted that as there is delay of nineteen years, the claim for MMCC to the widow and employment to the son 
could not be considered in their favour. 


13. I have perused the written statements of the union and management, evidence adduced and argument 
advanced by both parties. It is clear from the materials on the record that Budhani Mejhain, wife submitted an 
application dated 18.06.2001 for employment under ECL within a short period of time from the date of death of 
Mangru Majhi on 01.05.2001. The management did not consider her application. It is gathered from Exhibit M-5, a 
letter submitted by Budhani Mejhain dated 05.07.2016 before the Agent, Jambad OCP that it was on the instruction 
and advice of the Competent Authority of the ECL management she opted for monetary compensation instead of 
employment and explaining the delay she has clearly stated that the delay was not caused on her part, it was 
completely due to the time taken by ECL for observing the formalities. It appears from the materials on record that 
the management has delayed considerably in considering the application for employment of the dependent wife and 
at no point of time the management communicated to her that her prayer for employment was regretted. After 
waiting for a reasonable period, the dependent wife prayed for employment of her son on 22.08.2005 (Exhibit W-5). 
In the said application she stated that her son attained twenty years of age and he was in a position to perform any 
kind of job. The management did not consider the prayer for payment of MMCC as well as the claim for 
employment for more than ten years without any communication. For the first time on 08.02.2015 and thereafter on 
13.08.2015, 09.06.2016 and 16.01.2017 (Exhibit W-6 to W-9), management of Jambad OCP after intervals of a year 
at a time asked Chando Majhi to appear for his screening for his employment. It is strange to find that a screening 
test was arranged more than ten years after the application for employment was submitted, without any suitable 
explanation for this delay on the part of management. Budhani Mejhain was asked to appear before the Screening 
Committee for her monetary compensation only on 05.09.2019. Thereafter, she was informed by letter dated 
21.01.2021 that her prayer could not be considered for delay of long nineteen years. It is palpably clear that the 
entire delay has been caused by the lackadaisical approach of the management of ECL to deal with the circumstances 
and leaving the matter unresolved for several years. The dependents of the deceased cannot be held responsible for 
the delay. 


14. It is a settled principle of law that NCWA is a negotiated agreement that has been arrived at after collective 
deliberation between the employer and employees. It is a settlement under Section 2(p) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 and has binding effect on the parties thereto under Section 18 (3) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. The 
terms of compassionate employment in NCWA therefore have to be strictly construed and both the employee and the 
employees are bound by the terms of condition governing the terms of providing employment of the dependent of 
the deceased employee and the monetary compensation to be provided to the female dependent of the deceased 
employee. In the case of Sukumoni Hembram alias Sukumoni Mejhan Vs. Union of India and Others [MAT 27 
of 2024] the Hon’ble Division Bench of the Hon'ble High Court at Calcutta held : 


“ None of the clauses 9.3.0 to 9.5.0 of NCWA provide any time limit for applying or prescribe any financial solvency 
of the family as a disqualification. It does not allow the employer to choose between the dependents as to who it 
would grant the employment or the MMCC.” 


It also transpires from Point No. 2 of the Memorandum of Settlement dated 22.05.2007 arrived at between the 
management of ECL and its workmen that there is no time bar in the NCWA for considering the proposal for 
employment of the dependent. The management of ECL therefore without any deliberation with the concerned 
employees’ union cannot unilaterally declare that unless an application for employment is submitted within a 
specific time it would be considered as a time-barred application. In the present case the management of Jambad 
OCP fixed up four dates for screening of Chando Majhi for the purpose of considering the claim for employment 
made long before on 22.08.2005 (Exhibit W-5). The management could not explain the inordinate delay on their part 
in considering the prayer of the dependent of the deceased employee as per provisions of NCWA. On 21.01.2021 the 
Assistant Manager (Personnel), Jambad OCP informed Budhani Mejhain for the first time that her claim for 
MMCC was rejected on the ground of delay (Exhibit M-9). Budhani Mejhain submitted her application on 
05.07.2016 for monetary compensation (Exhibit M-5) she disclosed that she was opting for monetary compensation 
on the instruction and advice of Competent Authority of ECL. By letter dated 23/26.09.2019 (Exhibit W-3) 
management asked her for some documents. Therefore, rejecting the prayer for monetary compensation of the 
widow after delay on the part of management was grossly erroneous and illegal. 


15. In the present case the wife of the deceased submitted an application for her employment within two 
months from the date of death of her husband. Management neither processed her prayer for employment nor 
communicate anything to the widow in that matter to ameliorate the situation arising out of death of her husband. 
When Chando Majhi, who already figured in the SRE of the deceased employee and his age was recorded as two 
years as on 01.04.1987 (Exhibit W-11) attained majority, the wife of the deceased employee again submitted an 
application on 22.08.2005 for granting employment to her son. The dependents of the deceased employee could have 
been estopped from submitting a second claim for employment if the management acted on the basis of the first 
application submitted by the widow on 18.06.2001. The argument advanced by the management that the applications 


7556 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


for employment and MMCC could not be allowed due to delay therefore has no legs to stand and is not sustainable 
in view of the contributory negligence of management to sort out such pressing issues in a listless manner. 


16. The management of ECL has placed reliance upon the decision of the Hon'ble Supreme Court of India in 
the case of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd Vs. Anil Badyakar and Others [Civil Appeal No. 3597 of 2009]. Facts of 
the case in brief was that father-in-law of Anil Badyakar was an employee under ECL under Coal India Limited and 
died in harness in the year 1981. After a family settlement an application for employment was submitted in the 
year 1983 for compassionate appointment in favour of the son-in-law of the deceased employee. No objection was 
submitted by the legal heirs of the deceased. A Police Verification was made and after compliance of all formalities 
a letter of appointment was issued in favour of the petitioner in May 1993. Four months after joining his service the 
Director (Personnel) by order dated 23.09.1993 cancelled the provisional letter of appointment on the ground that it 
was a belated application, having been filed after a lapse of twelve years from the date of death of the employee. The 
son-in-law of the deceased filed a Writ Petition before the Hon’ble Sigle Judge which was allowed and the he was 
reinstated. An appeal was preferred before the Hon’ble Division Bench which was rejected. On the basis of Special 
Leave Petition (Civil) an appeal was preferred before the Hon'ble Supreme Court of India which was allowed and 
the Hon'ble Apex Court of India held that the decision of the employer was proper and the same should not have 
been interfered by the High Court. The fact and circumstances involved in the case of Anil Badyakar (Supra.) is 
distinguishable from the facts of the present case. In the instant case there is no delay over family settlement but 
delay cropped up due to utter inaction on the part of the management of ECL to appreciate the case of dependents of 
deceased employee. Therefore, the decision of Anil Badyakar (Supra.) case is not applicable under the fact and 
circumstances of this case. 


17. It needs to be borne in mind that compassionate appointment is an exception to the general rule of merit- 
based recruitment under Article 14 of the Constitution of India. Compassionate appointment is governed by the 
terms of appointment of the employees. It is not a matter of right unless it arises out of the terms of employment of 
the deceased employee which is laid down in the NCWA. Clause 9.3.0 to 9.5.0 of NCWA provide the terms and 
conditions for grant of employment to a dependent and monetary compensation to female dependent. None of such 
provisions prescribe that to avail the benefit of employment as a dependent, the financial condition of the 
deceased employee’s family has to be considered nor does such provisions lay down any time limit. Ordinary 
prudence suggests that such application should be filed and be dealt within reasonable period otherwise it loses its 
objective, efficiency and purpose. Since the dependents of a deceased employee are considered to have weak 
bargaining capacity than the management, the terms of settlement in NCWA needs to be interpreted in a manner 
which is beneficial to the dependents. The management therefore cannot be a party contributing to delay and 
thereafter be allowed to regret granting the relief on the same ground. 


18. Clause 9.3.0 of NCWA makes provision for employment to the dependent of an employee in case of 
permanent disability or death while in service of the company. Clause 9.3.2 deals with the employment or payment 
of monetary compensation to a female dependent which is governed under Clause 9.5.0. On a close reading of 
Clause 9.5.0 (iii) it appears that in case of death either in a mine accident or for other reasons or medical unfitness, if 
no employment has been offered and the male dependent of the concerned worker is twelve years and above in age, 
he will be kept on a live roster and would be provided employment commensurate with his skill and qualifications 
when he attains the age of 18 years. During the period the male dependent is on live roster, the female dependent 
will be paid monetary compensation as per rates at paras (i) & (ii) above. This will be effective from 01.01.2000. 
The application for employment of Chando Majhi, the dependent son was filed on 22.08.2005 (Exhibit W-5/M-2), 
where Budhani Mejhain stated that her son’s age at the relevant time was twenty years. Clause 9.5.0 of NCWA 
clearly states that if no employment is offered and male dependent is 12 (twelve) years or above, his name has to be 
maintained in the Live Roster. This is a duty cast upon the employer irrespective of any formal application is made 
by the dependent of the employee. The application for monetary compensation was submitted at a later period of 
time on 05.07.2016 (Exhibit M-5) at the instance of the management. Therefore, the dependent wife is entitled to 
monetary compensation from the date of death of her husband till employment is provided to the dependent son. The 
record reveals that Chando Majhi was two years of age on 01.04.1987, therefore he was a minor until 01.04.2003. It 
emerges from such fact and circumstances that Chando Majhi, the dependent son having claimed employment as a 
dependent is entitled to get employment under ECL on observance of all formalities. Budhani Mejhain, the widow of 
the deceased employee is entitled to Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation from 01.05.2001 to 31.03.2003 at the 
rate provided in the NCWA. The Industrial Dispute is allowed in favour of the dependents. 
Hence, 
ORDERED 

that the Industrial Dispute is allowed on contest in favour of the dependent son and wife of the Late Mangru 
Majhi, ex-employee of Jambad OCP under Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited. The management of Eastern 
Coalfields Limited is directed to provide employment to Chando Majhi, the dependent son of the deceased employee 
on observance of all formalities within two (2) months from the date of communication of the Award. Management 
of Eastern Coalfields Limited is also directed to pay Monthly Monetary Cash Compensation to Budhani Mejhain, the 
widow of the deceased employee from 01.05.2001 till employment is provided to her son as per provision of Clause 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7557 


9.5.0 at the admissible rate provided in the National Coal Wage Agreement. Let an award be drawn up in light of my 
above findings. Let copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New 
Delhi for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERIEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 17 दिसम्बर, 2025

सरकार बीसीसीएल के प्रबंधतंत्र के संबद्ध नियोजको और उनके कर्मकारों के बीच, अनुबंध में निर्दिष्ट औद्योगिक विवाद में

AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-20012/323/2001-IR(C-T) dated 27/09/2001 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Sijua Area of M/s. BCCL, Sijua, Dhanbad and their workman through Organizing 
Secretary, Rashtriya Colliery Mazdoor Sangh, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


2. On receiving order no. L-20012/323/2001-IR(C-I) dated 27/09/2001 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 222 of 2001 was registered on 01.11.2001 and 


7558 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


thereafter the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along 
with relevant documents and witnesses in support of their claims. 


3. After issuance of regd. notice, none appeared on behalf of both the parties. It further transpires from the case 
record, when the case record was put up after a long gap on 17.09.2021, registered notices were sent to both the 
parties but even thereafter no step was taken from both sides. Though on 04.07.2025 and 12.09.2025, Sri D.K. 
Verma, Advocate for the management appeared but union/workman did not appear before this Tribunal. 


4. On perusal of the entire case record it transpires that the workman never appeared before this Tribunal for a 
period of 24 years which shows that the workman has lost his interest in this case and therefore, for the ends of 
justice, this case deserves to be dismissed for non prosecution. 


5. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed for non prosecution and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the 
above reference case. Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and notification.

BCCL. and their workmen received by the Central Government on 17/12/2025. 


[No. L-20012/456/1998 — IR (CM-D] 
SALONI, Dy. Director/Link officer 
ANNEXURE

Presiding Officer 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7559 


Appearances: 
For the Employers :-Sri D.K. Verma, Ld. Advocate 


For the workmen :- Sri Manas Chatterjee, Representative 
State : Jharkhand. Industry:-Coal 
Dated 10/12/2025 
AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub —section (2A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947) Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-20012/456/1998-IR(C-I) dated 04/06/1999 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer 1.6. management of Bhatdeeh Colliery of M/s. BCCL, Dhanbad and their workman through Area 
Secretary, Bihar Colliery Kamgar Union, Bhatdeeh, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the action of the management of BCCL, Bhatdeeh Colliery in denying employment to 
Sh. Shambhu Kumar Bhuia claiming to be the dependent son of Late Sh. ChandrikaBhuia, general Mazdoor 
is justified? If not, to what relief the said Sh. Shambhu Kr. Bhuia is entitled?” 


2: The case of the claimant as per the written statement filed on 08.01.2002 is that Late Chandrika Bhuia had 
been working as permanent workman at Bhatdeeh Colliery since long with unblemished record of service. He died 
on 06.01.1993 during the tenure of his service. The dependent son of Late Chandrika Bhuia namely Sri Shambhu 
Kumar Bhuia represented before the management for his employment as per provision of NCWA but the 
management neither provided him employment nor disposed of his representation by passing any speaking order. Sri 
Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is the only son of Late Chandrika Bhuia and he was /is wholly dependent on Late Chandrika 
Bhuia. The NCWA is a settlement and as per the provision of the same the dependent son of Late Chandrika Bhuia 
is entitled for employment. Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia had submitted before the management authentic documents 
in support of his relation with Late Chandrika Bhuia and also completed all formalities for the dependent 
employment but the management due to anti-labour policy, did not take any step for providing him employment and 
seeing no other alternative the union raised an industrial dispute before the ALC(C), Dhanbad but the same ended in 
failure due to the adamant attitude of the management. The Govt. of India referred the dispute for adjudication 
before this Tribunal. The action of the management in denying employment to Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia, 
dependent son of Late Chandrika Bhuia was not justified and against the principle of natural justice, so prayed to 
pass award in his favour. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per written statement filed on 28.08.2002 is that the 
present reference is not maintainable. Late Chandrika Bhuia was working as a General Mazdoor of Bhatdeeh 
Colliery and he died on 06.01.1993 as per Certificate issued by the doctor of Bhatdeeh Colliery. Late Chandrika 
Bhuia was issued service excerpt while he was alive but he did not return the same to the management. Late 
Chandrika Bhuia during his service period did not make any declaration about his family details, whether he had 
wife or son. One Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia claimed for the employment saying that he is the son of Late Chandrika 
Bhuia but he failed to produce all the relevant documents regarding his claim and accordingly the claim of Sri 
Shambhu Kumar Bhuia for dependent employment is false. The Area Secretary, BCKU raised an Industrial Dispute 
for dependent employment vide letter dated 16.12.1996 before the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Dhanbad 
and on receipt of the letter from the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Dhanbad, the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR), 
Western Jharia Area vide his letter dated 15.07.1998 explained the facts of the case and prayed for closing the 
matter. As the said Shambhu Kumar Bhuia failed to produce all relevant documents pertaining to his claim as asked 
by the management, the management was fully justified in denying the employment to Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia 
and at last it was prayed to reject the claim of applicant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia. 


A rejoinder dated 11.12.2002 has also been filed in reply to the written statement of the management by 
the workman in which he has denied all the statements made by the management in W/S claiming that it is 
absolutely false to allege that Late Chandrika Bhuia did not return the service excerpt and he did not make any 
declaration about his family details and also that admittedly Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is the son of Late 
ChandrikaBhuia. 


4. Likewise a separate rejoinder on behalf of the management has also been filed on 29.08.2002 to the W/S 
filed on behalf of the workman in which the entire claim of the workman has been denied by the management 
claiming that the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has failed to produce the relevant documents to prove that he is 
the son of Late Chandrika Bhuia. 


5. In support of his case the claimant has examined WW-1 Shambhu Kumar Bhuia, Claimant himself. 


6. By way of documentary evidence the following documents have been adduced from the side of the 
claimant:- 


7560 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


Exhibit W-1 — Verification Roll 
Exhibit W-2 — Identification Certificate 
Exhibit W-3 — Hand written application dated 25.03.1993 of the claimant Shambhu Bhuia for employment 


Exhibit W-4 - Letter of Assistant Commissioner Gr.II dated 01.06.2004 to the Dy. CPM, Bhatdeeh Colliery, 
Dhanbad 


Exhibit W-5 — Death Certificate of Chandrika Bhuia 

Exhibit W-6 — Family Certificate 

Exhibit W-7 — Letter of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia dated 01.10.1993 to the Project Officer. 

Exhibit W-8 — Letter of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia to the Regional Labour Commissioner(C) Dhanbad. 
‘Te In support of its case the management has examined MW-1 Amarnath Pandey. 

8. The management has adduced the following documentary evidence:- 

Exhibit M-1 — Application Form Proforma-I 


Exhibit M-2 — Letter of BCKU, Area Secretary to the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Dhanbad dated 
16.12.1996. 


Exhibit M-3 — Letter of Project Officer dated 20/21.06.1997 to the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR). 

Exhibit M-4 — Letter dated 06/08.09.1997 of the Project Officer to the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR). 

Exhibit M-5 — Letter dated 15.07.1998 of the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR) to the Assistant Labour Commissioner. 
Exhibit M-6 — Letter dated 09/10.06.1997 of Dy. Personnel Manager (IR) to the Project Officer. 

9. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


10. The Representative on behalf of the claimant has submitted that Late Chandrika Bhuia was an employee of 
Bhatdeeh Colliery of W.J. Area of BCCL and he died on 06.01.1993 leaving behind his only son Shambhu Kumar 
Bhuia. His wife had already died on 18.06.1978. Thereafter Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia applied for employment in 
place of his father Late Chandrika Bhuia under the provisions of NCWA but unfortunately the management did not 
consider the same stating that the name of Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia did not exist in the service excerpts of his 
father and also that he has not submitted the complete required papers for employment in place of his father. He has 
further submitted that Late Chandrika Bhuia was not educated and due to lack of knowledge he could not get the 
name of his son Shambhu Kumar Bhuia inserted in his service excerpt. The claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is 
entitled for employment in place of his father for which he has submitted family details as well as identification 
certificate and verification roll and prayed to pass award in his favour. 


11. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel of the management has submitted that Late Chandrika Bhuia never 
submitted his family details before the management and as such the management is not aware of the facts that 
Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is the son of Late Chandrika Bhuia. The claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has also failed to 
produce the relevant documents pertaining to his claim to prove his bonfide claim for employment and therefore his 
claim was legally rejected by the management and prayed to reject the reference. 


12. Issue 


Whether the action of the management of BCCL, Bhatdeeh Colliery in denying employment to 
Sh.Shambhu Kumar Bhuia claiming to be the dependent son of Late Sh. Chandrika Bhuia, General Mazdoor is 
justified? If not, to what relief the said Sh. Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is entitled? 


13. To establish his claim, WW-1 Shambhu Kumar Bhuia, the claimant himself, has deposed that his father 
Late Chandrika Bhuia was working as General Mazdoor in Bhatdeeh Colliery and he died on 06.01.1993 during 
tenure of his service and thereafter he was left alone in his family as his mother had already died prior to the death of 
his father. He has further stated that after the death of his father he had submitted his application for the employment 
before the management and also submitted all his documents before the BCCL but he was not provided the 
employment. After the death of his father he was paid gratuity of his father. He is the only son of his father Late 
Chandrika Bhuia. In his cross-examination he has stated that he does not know whether his father had got recorded 
his name in his service record. He was dependent on his father and certificate of the same was also submitted by him 
in the Office of BCCL. 


14. Now as regards the documentary evidence adduced on behalf of the claimant, on perusal of Exhibit W-1 it 
transpires that this is a verification roll in which the full details of Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has been given and his 
certified photo has also been affixed therein by Mukhiya Panchayat. In this verification roll the father’s name of the 
claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has been mentioned as Late Chandrika Bhuia. Exhibit W-2 is the Identification 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7561 


Certificate which also bears full details of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia and in this document also the father’s 
name of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has been mentioned as Late Chandrika Bhuia and on the back side of 
the same, attested photograph of the claimant has been affixed that also bears the stamp and signature of Panchayat 
Mukhiya Narayan Singh as well as signature and stamp of BDO. Exhibit W-3 is the application dated 25.03.1993 
submitted by the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia before the Project Officer, Bhatdeeh Colliery and on its perusal it 
transpires that he has submitted this application for dependent employment as well as payment of entire dues as per 
NCWA. Exhibit W-4 is the letter of Assistant Commissioner Gr. II dated 1/16.06.2004 written to the Dy. CPM, 
Bhatdeeh Colliery and on its perusal it transpires that the full details as per service record of Chandrika Bhuia, his 
family declaration letter issued under the signature of BDO, date of appointment etc. has been called for. Exhibit W- 
5 is the Death Certificate of Chandrika Bhuia dated 06/13.01.1993 and on its perusal it transpires that Chandrika 
Bhuia, General Mazdoor, Bhatdeeh Colliery expired on 06.01.1993 at 1:00 am. Exhibit W-6 is the Family 
Certificate and on its perusal it transpires that Chandrika Bhuia died on 06.01.1993, his father Bhatu Bhuia died on 
06.03.1975, his mother Kagwa Bhuini died on 11.12.1977, Sonia Bhuini the wife of Late Chandrika Bhuia died on 
18.06.1978 and Shambhu Kumar Bhuia, son of Late Chandrika Bhuia is the only leaving member in the family of 
Late Chandrika Bhuia and the certificate has also been stamped and signed by the Block Development Officer, 
Lakhisarai as well as Ex-Mukhiya, Narayan Singh. Exhibit W-7 is the letter of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia 
dated 01.10.1993 written to Project Officer, Bhatdeeh Colliery and on its perusal it transpires that the claimant has 
replied to the letter of BCCL dated 23/26.08.1993 regarding not submitting the service excerpts or any family list by 
the Late Chandrika Bhuia in the office of BCCL. The claimant has stated that his father was suffering from T.B and 
due to the same he did not consult with any one and so he had no knowledge about the same and the claimant has 
further claimed that he is the only son of Late Chandrika Bhuia regarding which he has submitted the documents 
issued by the Mukhiya as well as BDO. Exhibit W-8 is the letter of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia written to 
the Regional Labour Commissioner (Central), Dhanbad and on its perusal it transpires that he had submitted his 
claim for payment of gratuity after the death of his father and the management has deposited Rs. 22822 as per 
NCWA-IV that was paid to him but he is entitled to payment of Rs. 32561.55 as gratuity on the basis of NCWA-V 
and has prayed to pass order in this regard and give direction to the agent Bhatdeeh Colliery. 


15. Now as regards the oral testimony of MW-1 Amarnath Pandey, Dy. Manager Personnel, 
Bhatdeeh Colliery Murlidih, Dhanbad, he has deposed that Chandrika Bhuia was employee of Bhatdeeh Colliery and 
he died on 06.01.1993 while in service. Thereafter Shambhu Kumar Bhuia claiming himself to be his son, submitted 
an application for employment in place of his father before the office but he had not submitted the entire documents 
and his name was also not recorded as dependent of Chandrika Bhuia in his office and therefore, his application was 
not considered by the office. In his cross-examination he has stated that in the year 1987, the service excerpts of 
Chandrika Bhuia was made available to him with direction to fill up the same and submit before the office but the 
office does not have the receipt of handing over the service excerpts to Chandrika Bhuia. He does not have any 
knowledge whether the office had issued any reminder in this regard to Chandrika Bhuia or not. In the office record 
it has not been mentioned as to which documents were not submitted by the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia. 


16. As regards the documentary evidence of management, Exhibit M-1 is the Application Form 
Proforma-I of the deceased Chandrika Bhuia that has full details of Chandrika Bhuia as well as the claimant 
Shambhu Kumar Bhuia and in the relationship column Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has been shown as the son of Late 
Chandrika Bhuia. It further transpires that the photograph of claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia that has been affixed 
on this application form, is attested by the Mukhiya Panchayat, Narayan Singh under his seal and signature and it 
also bears the seal and signature of BDO. Exhibit M-2 is the letter of BCKU, Area Secretary dated 16.12.1996 to 
the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Dhanbad and on its perusal it transpires that it has been written for initiation 
of conciliation proceedings regarding non employment to the dependent of Late Chandrika Bhuia. Exhibit M-3 is 
the letter of Project Officer, Bhatdeeh Colliery dated 20/21.6.1997 to the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR) and on its 
perusal it transpires that by this letter he has been informed that Chandrika Bhuia had died on 06.01.1993 as per the 
Certificate issued by Dy. M.S. of Bhatdee Colliery and Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia claimed for employment and 
other legal dues as son of late Chandrika Bhuia and while processing the case of employment as dependent of late 
Chandrika Bhuia, some irregularities have been found in the service file of Late Chandrika Bhuia. The service 
excerpt was issued to the workman while he was alive but he did not return the same and during his service period 
he did not declare his family details or nominee in Form ‘F’. It has further been mentioned that as per Form-‘B’ his 
name appeared on 81. No. 268, his father name has been given as BhatuBhuia, his age has been written as 38 years 
as on 19.12.1987, his date of appointment is 17.10.1971 and his home address is Khairi, P.O. Lakhisarai, Dist- 
Munger. Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia is the son of Late Chandrika Bhuia as per identification and verification 
certificate issued by the BDO of Lakhisarai (Mungyr) Block. He had sought for clarification from Area office 
whether the said dependent may be considered for employment or legal dues or not vide Note-sheet No. 94/1159 
dated 23.06.1994 but no clarification has been received and to avoid legal complications, they have deposited the 
gratuity of late Chandrika Bhuia to the ALC (C) cum controlling authority on 06.09.1996. Ext.M 4 is the letter of 
Project Officer dated 06/08.09.1997 written to the Dy. Personnel Manager (IR) in which he has been requested to 
examine the case file of the dependent employment of Late Chandrika Bhuia. It has also been mentioned therein that 
during his lifetime, Chandrika Bhuia did not make any declaration that he had a wife or son and there is lurking 


7562 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


doubt in the genuinity of Shambhu Kumar Bhuia who is claiming to be a son of Late Chandrika Bhuia and this is 
creating problem in processing the employment papers. Exhibit M-5 is the letter dated 15.07.1998 of Dy. Personnel 
Manager (IR) to the Assistant Labour Commissioner (C), Dhanbad regarding I.D. over non-employment to the 
dependent of Late Chandrika Bhuia in which it has been commented that Late Chandrika Bhuia, Ex-employee of 
Bhatdih Colliery did not make any declaration that he had a wife or son and Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has claimed 
for employment saying that he is the son of Late Chandrika Bhuia but he has failed to produce all relevant 
documents pertaining to his claim and it has been requested to close the case there being no merit in the case. 
Exhibit M-6 is the letter of Dy. Personnel Manager (IR) dated 09/10.06.1997 written to the Project Officer, Bhatdih 
Colliery in which it has been stated that the comments in the I.D. Case file no. 1/2/97-E-IV dated 22.1.1997 over 
non-employment of dependent of Late Chandrika Bhuia has not been received as yet and he has been requested for 
the same. 


17. On perusal of the oral as well as documentary evidence adduced from both sides it is evident that Late 
Chandrika Bhuia, General Mazdoor of Bhatdih Colliery expired on 06.01.1993. It has also been admitted from the 
side of the management and thereafter it further transpires that the management is claiming that in the office of the 
BCCL there is no service excerpts of the Late Chandrika Bhuia nor there is any declaration of Chandrika Bhuia 
regarding his family members and so there is no official record with BCCL to prove that the claimant is the son of 
Late Chandrika Bhuia and so his application was not considered whereas after the death of Late Chandrika Bhuia, 
the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia has submitted his claim application for dependent employment in place of his 
father and so the main issue in this case now is if there is no office record under BCCL regarding the names of 
family members such as his son or wife, can the claim of Shambhu Kumar Bhuia who is claiming himself as son of 
Late Chandrika Bhuia be legally considered by the management for dependent employment or not? The answer of 
this Tribunal is that in this situation also the son can still be entitled to dependent employment provided he is a 
genuine dependent family member and fulfils all other eligibility criteria under specific scheme of the NCWA. The 
non- mentioning of the only son’s name in the service record of the Late workman for whatsoever reason, cannot be 
an automatic bar to his son’s claim for dependent employment. In this regard it is also relevant to mention that the 
core objective of dependent employment is to provide immediate financial assistance to the family in distress due to 
the death of the workman. Here in the instant case, the claimant has very clearly stated in his cross-examination as 
१४५७४] that he was dependent on his father and as per his statement in his examination-in-chief, his mother had 
already died and thus he was only member left in his family after the death of his father. 


18. Now as regards the relationship of the claimant Shambhu Kumar Bhuia to the late Chandrika Bhuia in the 
prevailing facts and circumstances of this case, on perusal of Exhibit W-6 which is family certificate it is crystal 
clear that Late Chandrika Bhuia died on 06.01.1993 and his father had already died on 06.03.1975, his mother had 
already died on 11.12.1977 and his wife Sonia Bhuini had already died on 18.06.1978 and the claimant Shambhu 
Kumar Bhuia who has been described as the son of Late Chandrika Bhuia, aged above 25 years has been shown to 
be the son and the only alive family member of Late Chandrika Bhuia and this document bears the stamp and 
signature of the BDO, Lakhisarai as well as the signature of Ex-Mukhiya Panchayat namely Narayan Singh and for 
this reason, the genuineness of this document cannot be questioned/doubted in any way and if the management raises 
any doubt on the genuineness of this document, the onus to prove the same otherwise, lies on the management but he 
has not produced any document contrary to the same. Not only this, the other documents such as Exhibit W-1, 
Exhibit W-2 as well as the document submitted by the management i.e. Exhibit M-1 which bears the seal and 
signature of BDO as well as Panchayat Mukhia also goes to prove the relationship of the claimant as son of Late 
Chandrika Bhuia. Most importantly, Exhibit W-8 as well as the oral testimony of WW-1 Shambhu Kumar Bhuia 
makes it abundantly clear that the gratuity of the deceased workman Chandrika Bhuia has been paid to the claimant 
Shambhu Kumar Bhuia which leaves no doubt that he is the son and only alive family member of the deceased 
Chandrika Bhuia and so considering the above facts and circumstances as well as the evidence available on the case 
record this Tribunal finds and holds that the action of the management of BCCL, Bhatdeeh Colliery in denying 
employment to Sri Shambhu Kumar Bhuia, claiming to be the dependent son of Late ChandrikaBhuia, General 
Mazdoor, is not at all justified and he is entitled to the dependent employment in place of his father Late Chandrika 
Bhuia and the management is accordingly directed to provide him employment within a period of three months from 
the date of the publication of this Award. The reference is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of 
India, New Delhi for information and notification.

2. The case of the workmen as per the written statement filed on their behalf on 25.08.2015 is that 
Sri BeniMadhavYadav and 41 others have been working at SwangWashery since the year 2002 continuously and 
regularly. The concerned workmen have been working within the precinct and premises of the washery in permanent 
and prohibited category of job since long with unblemished record of service. They have been working regularly 
under the direct control and supervision of the management. All the implement for execution of the job are being 
supplied by the management. The workmen have been rendering services and producing goods for the benefit of the 
management. So they are employees of the management and the management have been paying them wages in the 
name of different intermediaries to camouflage the real issue. The management is neither paying the concerned 
workmen wages and other benefits as per NCWA nor regularized their services. They represented before the 
management several times for their regularization and wages as per NCWA but till today without any effect. The 
management have been exploiting the poor workmen by taking advantage of general un-employed condition. The 
management have been playing all kind of tactics to conceal the real fact and to camouflage the real issue. The 
repeated demand of regularization have annoyed the management to the extent of threatening the workmen to stop 
them from work. After exhausting all the avenue for settlement the union on behalf of the workmen raised an 
Industrial Dispute before the ALC(C), Hazaribagh along with names, father’s name, address and photograph duly 
attested by the union and the management appeared in the conciliation proceedings and taken the plea that no 
employer-employee relationship exists between the workmen and the management. The workmen are the members 
of Provident fund and possessing documents in support of their work until the management refused to settle the issue 
amicably, resulting in failure of conciliation. The appropriate Govt. after appreciating the merit of the case referred 
the dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal. The action of the management in not regularizing the services of the 
workmen as plant cleaning mazdoor is neither legal nor justified rather illegal and arbitrary and against the principles 
of natural justice and so prayed to answer the reference in favour of the workmen by directing the management to 
regularize the services of the workmen with retrospective effect with all arrears of wages and attendant benefits. 


3. On the other hand the case of the management as per the written statement cum rejoinder filed on 22.12.2015 is 
that the reference is not maintainable either in law or in facts. The term of reference is without application of mind. It 
is well settled principle of law that only the Tribunal has jurisdiction under section 11 of the Industrial Dispute Act, 
1947 to decide, whether the person concerned have been performing the permanent and perennial job for the last 
several years or not. It has further been submitted that the persons concerned in the present reference have never 
been engaged by the management of SawangWashery under plant cleaning job. No employer-employee relationship 
exists between the management and the person concerned. The demand of regularization can not be termed as an 
Industrial Dispute within the meaning of Section 2(k) of the I.D. Act 1947 as much as the term industrial dispute 
under the said provision of the Act means the dispute which is connected with the employment or non-employment 
or the terms of employment with the conditions of labour. The persons concerned do not fall under the category of 
workman. M/s. BCCL under which the SwangWashery under Kathara Area falls being a government company 
within the meaning of Section 617 of the company’s Act and that being the position the management has to give 
equal opportunity to all the job seekers of the company. The sponsoring union enrolled the name of job seekers and 
raised the present industrial dispute to get employment in the company in the guise of regularization through back 
door. For employment in the company, the management has to notify the vacancy and call for the eligible candidates 
for selection from amongst the eligible job seekers otherwise it would amount to the violation of Articles 14 and 16 
of the Constitution of India. The Hon’ble Supreme Court of India in case of Uma Devi has deprecated regularization 
terming the same as back door entry into the public employment further holding that the ad-hoc, casual, temporary 
and contractual workers have got no legal right over the post, as such, question of their regularization does not arise. 
During the conciliation proceeding, the union made out their case on the basis of identity cards of the concerned 
person which was alleged to be issued by the contractor Sri Anil Kumar, who has no authority to issue identity card 
on behalf of the management. The alleged identity card is forged and fabricated documents and no reliance can be 
placed on the same. The contractor Sri Anil Kumar was never awarded any kind of work which is permanent and 
perennial in nature. The work order were issued for short term for re-handling spillage materials by the work orders 
dated 03.01.2009, 07.03.2009, 08.12.2009, 03.12.2009, 17.11.2009, 25.12.2008, 22,07.2010 and 17.08.2010 and for 
which he was paid consolidated amounts. In rejoinder it has been stated that the statements made in Para-1 of the 
W/S of the union is not correct. It is relevant to mention that BeniMadhav and 41 others were never engaged and 
appointed by the management of SwangWashery since 2002. The statement made in Para-2 of the W/S is not 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7571 


relevant. The management awarded some work to the contractor Sri Anil Kumar. The statement made in Para-3 of 
the W/S is not correct. The management never awarded permanent and perennial nature of job to any contractor. The 
statement made in Para-4 and 5 of the W/S is not correct. The contractor is responsible for execution of work which 
was awarded to him and the workmen engaged by him used to work under his direct control and supervision. The 
statement made in Para-6 of the W/S is not relevant. The statement made in Para-7, 8, 9 and 10 of the W/S is not 
correct. The allegation leveled against the management by the union is false and baseless. The Government 
Company has never involved in camouflaging the real issue. No employer-employee relationship exists between the 
management and the concerned persons and no question of their regularization arise. The statement made in Para-11, 
12, 13, 14, 15 and 16 are neither correct nor relevant. The government can not decide the merit of the case. The term 
of reference is prejudicial to the management and the persons concerned are not entitled to any relief and prayed to 
answer the reference accordingly. 


4. Rejoinder has also been filed on behalf of the workmen to the W/S of the management in which the 
workmen have denied the entire claims of the management made in his W/S submitting that there is employer- 
employee relationship between the management and the workmen and the workmen have been doing continuously 
the permanent and prohibited category of job but the management has not maintained statutory documents for the 
same. They have been issued identity cards by the competent person of the management and prayed to hold that the 
demand of the workmen are legal and justified. 


5. In support of their case the following witnesses have been examined from the side of the workmen: - 
WW-1, Shyam SundarYadav and WW-2, Anil Kumar. 

6. The following documents have been adduced from the side of the workmen:- 

Exhibit W-1 — Letter dated 12.10.2013 of the Project Officer 

Exhibit W-2 — Letter dated 4/6.10.2013 written by the Project Officer 

Exhibit W-3 — Engagement of six workmen in plant cleaning by the management dated 08.10.2015 

Exhibit W-4 — Regarding Identity Card 

Exhibit W-5 Series— Attendance Register 


Exhibit W-6 Series— Vocational Training Certificate, Office Order dated 08.03.2015, Annual P.F. Account 
Statement, Identity Cards 


Exhibit W-7 — Photo copy of engagement slip 

Exhibit W-7/1 — Original Copy of engagement slip 

Exhibit W-8 — Deployment of cleaning workers. 

7. On the other hand the management has examined MW-1, Raman Kumar. 
8. The management has adduced the following documentary evidence:- 
Exhibit M-1 Series —Copies of work order issued to the different contractors. 

9. Heard both sides and perused the materials on record. 


The Ld. Counsel on behalf of the workmen has submitted that the workmen have been doing the job of plant 
cleaning mazdoor since the year 2002 under the direct control and supervision of the management and this type of 
job is permanent and prohibited category of jobs and so after the central government notification for prohibition of 
contract labour, they are entitled for regularization. The workmen have been given vocational training by the 
management as per Vocational Training Rules and they have also been issued identity cards etc by the competent 
authority. They have completed more than 240 days of attendance in every calendar year and there is employer- 
employee relationship between the workmen and the management and though the management is taking the work 
from the workmen at par with that of the regular employees of the management but they are not being paid the 
wages as well as other facilities like them and therefore they have demanded for their regularization and prayed to 
pass award in favour of the concerned workmen. 


10. On the other hand the Ld. Counsel on behalf of the management has submitted that the concerned workmen 
have been working under the contractor Baleshwar Yadav and thus they are the contractor employees so not entitled 
for regularization. They have not been given appointment letter and no any pay slip or ID Cards from the side of the 
management and they have been doing the job under the contractor that were awarded to him under the work order 
that he has also submitted before the Court and lastly submitted that the contractors employees are not automatically 
entitled to the regularization after notification of prohibition of prohibited category of job. 


7572 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA DECEMBER 27, 2025/PAUSHA 6, 1947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)] 


11. Issue 


Whether the action of the Management of SwangWashery of CCL in not regularizing the services of 
S/ShriBeniMadhavYadav and 41 others as Plant Cleaning Mazdoors, who have been performing the permanent and 
perennial job for the last several years is fair and justified? If not, to what relief the concerned workman are entitled 
to? 


12. In this regard WW-1 ShyamSundarYadav who is one of the workmen has deposed that they have been 
working as Plant Cleaning Mazdoor since 2002 regularly and continuously under the direct control and supervision 
of the management. The plant cleaning job is of permanent and prohibited category of jobs and for doing of the same 
the management used to supply them all the implements for execution of the jobs and rendering their services and 
they have been producing goods for the benefits of the management. For all practical purpose they are the employees 
of the management but the management is paying them the wages below the rates of NCWA in the name of different 
intermediaries to camouflage the real issue. They have been performing permanent nature of job at par with other 
similarly situated workmen. They have also represented before the management several times for payment of wages 
but the management instead of redressing their legal demands, have been threatening to terminate their services. 
They have also submitted 6 items of documents. They have filed attendance register which bears the signature of 
management officials, Mines Vocational Training Certificate issued by the management as well as identity cards 
with the signature of management officials which shows that they have been continuously working in permanent and 
prohibited category of the jobs and the management camouflaging their entire true facts to deny their claims for 
regularization and their demands for regularization as Plant Cleaning Mazdoor with retrospective effect with all 
arrears of wages are legal and justified and the action of the management denying the same is illegal and unjustified. 
In the cross-examination he has stated that he is not working under any contractor. He has filed Identity Card issued 
to them by the management. He has denied that he has not filed I.D. Card issued by the management. He has also 
denied that they were working under Contractor Baleshwar Thakur. He has further denied that their work was not 
permanent nor perennial in nature though he has admitted that no appointment letter was issued to them nor any pay 
slip was issued. 


13. Likewise WW-2 Anil Kumar has also deposed the same facts as above stated by the WW-1 in his 
examination- in- chief. In the cross-examination he has stated that he cannot produce any documents that he was 
engaged by the management company. The I.D. Card issued to him bears the name of Baleshwar Thakur Contractor. 


14. Now as regards the documentary evidence of the workmen, Exhibit W-1 is letter dated 12.10.2013 written 
by the Project Officer, SwangWashery to the Working President of R.C.M.S., Kathara Area mentioning that a photo 
copy of the list of the contractor workers deployed by the contractors in SwangWashery is being made available and 
thereafter it further transpires that there are the names of 44 workers in the list. Exhibit W-2 is the letter dated 
4/6.10.2013 written by the Project Officer, SwangWashery to Sri MukeshYadav& 41 other contractor workers of Sri 
Baleshwar Thakur and on its perusal it transpires that this is regarding non-payment of wages & self immolation and 
it has been assured by this letter that they will be made payment by 2 PM next day and the fund has been 
communicated and they have been requested to call back self immolation and maintain amicable relationship with 
the management. Exhibit W-3 is the photo copy of a document of the engagement of 6 workmen in Plant Cleaning 
by the management dated 08.10.2015 under the signature of surveyor, Swang(w). Exhibit W-4 is the photo copy of 
a document regarding Identity Card of Shyam SundarYadav, ShyamSundarPaswan and Anil Yadav and the 
Certificate has been given by Assistant Manager, SwangWashery CCL that Identity Card of the above mentioned 
contractor workers are in process. Exhibit W-5 series is the photo copy of attendance register that has been marked 
as Exhibit with objection and on its perusal it transpires that this is photo copy of the attendance register from 
November 2007 to April 2010 and it is also evident from this attendance register that the attendance of the workmen 
in all the months are more than 240 days in a year. Exhibit W-6 Series are the photo copies of Vocational Training 
Certificate, Office Order, Annual PF Statement as well as Identity Cards of the concerned workmen and these have 
also been marked with objection. On its perusal it transpires that the workmen have undergone training required 
under chapter III of the Mines Vocational Training Rules, 1966 for the employment in a mine on surface and in open 
cast workings. It further appears that these training certificates for employment have also been signed and stamped 
by the Training Officer as well as counter signed and stamped by the Sr. Manager (E&M) Swang Washery. The 
office order dated 08.03.2015 shows that five workers have been nominated to attend the basic Training w.e-f. 
09.03.2015 to 19.03.2015 at Vocational Training Centre Jarangdih and this letter has been issued by the Project 
Officer, Swang Washery. The photo copy of the PF deduction shows that their contributions have been deposited 
under Employees Provident Fund Scheme, 1952. On perusal of the Identity Cards of the workmen it transpires that it 
has been issued from the Central Coal Field Ltd., SwangWashery, Kathara Area, Dist- Bokaro (Jharkhand)in the 
name of the concerned workmen as well as their father’s name, designation as mazdoor and the present address with 
the signature of the Issuing Authority as well as the card holder though it also bears the name of Contractor as 
Baleshwar Thakur. On perusal of Exhibit W-7 and W-7/1 it transpires that both these exhibits are nothing but the 
repetition of Exhibit W-3. Exhibit W-8 is the photo copy of the document of deployment of cleaning workers list 
dated 02.08.2015 of the SwangWashery in which the name of the Supervisor as Jai NarainMahto/Bijay Singh, 
Madan Singh/Jagdish Prasad have been mentioned and it is evident from this document that these concerned workers 


[भाग 1---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : दिसम्बर 27, 2025/पौष 6, 1947 7573 


have been working under the supervision of the management of SwangWashery. 


15. Now as regards the oral testimony of MW-1 Raman Kumar, he is the Project Officer of the 
SwangWashery of M/s. CCL and he has deposed that the management of SwangWashery never appointed 
BeniMadhav and 41 others as Plant Cleaning Mazdoor. No employer employee relationship exists between the 
management and the person concerned. He has further deposed that the union produced certain identity cards alleged 
to be issued by the contractor but the contractors have no authority to issue identity cards on behalf of the 
management and the alleged identity cards are forged and fabricated documents. The alleged photo copy of 
attendance register produced by the union is also fabricated documents. The contractor Sri Anil Kumar and others 
were never awarded any kind of work which is permanent and perennial in nature by the management of 
SwangWashery but in cross-examination he has admitted that the plant cleaning work is permanent and prohibitory 
category of work and he cannot say whether the concerned workmen have been working continuously under them or 
not. He has also denied that all have been given vocational training though he has admitted that the certificates filed 
have been issued by his institute. 


16. On perusal of Exhibit M-1 series it transpires that page no. | and 2 of this series are repetition. On perusal 
of all the pages of this series it transpires that the contractor Anil Kumar has been awarded work order (Civil) on 
different dates for clean coal, wash coal (P) belt conveyours and front drain of main washing of SGW as well as re- 
handling spilage materials from different parts of P.C.H, reclamation of spilage materials, replacement of spilage 
material and from its perusal it does not appears that it is related to the concerned workmen though the management 
has tried to prove that after getting award by the contractor Anil Kumar, his work was completed by the contractor 
employees and secondly, the work order bears the name of contractor as Anil Kumar whereas the Identity Card of 
the concerned workmen bears the name of contractor as Baleshwar Thakur and thus it also does not tally creating 
doubt on the stand of the management. 


17. On perusal of the entire materials on record adduced from both sides, oral as well as documentary evidence, 
this Tribunal finds that the concerned workmen were contract workers of Baleshwar Thakur (Exhibit W-5, Exhibit 
W-6 and Exhibit W-8)and they were working as Plant Cleaning Mazdoor which is a permanent and prohibited 
category of work (admitted by MW-1 in cross examination)and engaging contract labour for work i.e. permanent 
and perennial in nature is prohibited under the Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act, 1970. The Hon’ble 
Supreme Court have also consistently held in its judgments that the workers performing such tasks cannot be treated 
as contractual to deny them benefits and at the same time on perusal of Exhibit W-5 it is established that the 
concermed workmen have worked for more than 240 days in every calendar year which further strengthens the claim 
of the workmen as it indicates a continuous need for the work and not temporary and casual requirements. While 240 
days rule does not automatically grant permanency to a contract worker but it is a significant factor in establishing 
that the work itself is not casual rather permanent. The issuance of identity cards and providing vocational training 
(Exhibit W-6 series) as well as maintaining supervision of the work of the concerned workmen by deputing 
supervisors namely Jai NarainMahto/Bijay Singh, Madan Singh/Jagdish Prasad(Exhibit W-8) establishes that 
employer employee relationship exists between the concerned workmen and the management and thus introduction 
of the contractor Baleshwar Thakur between the management and the concerned workmen is nothing but sham 
because the work is permanent and perennial in nature and the purported contract system is merely a device and the 
lack of payment as per NCWA to the concerned workmen further reinforces the case of wrongful denial of the wages 
and exploitation under camouflage arrangements. 


18. Thus on the basis of above discussions or facts, circumstances and evidences this Tribunal finds and holds 
that the action of the management of SwangWashery of CCL in not regularizing the services of Sri 
BeniMadhavYadav and 41 others as Plant Cleaning Mazdoor who have been performing the permanent and 
perennial job for the last several years is neither fair nor justified and the concerned workmen are entitled to 
regularization and accordingly the management of CCL is directed to regularize the services of the concerned 
workmen with retrospective effect with all consequential benefits within a period of 3 months from the date of the 
publication of the Award by the Central Government, Ministry of Labour &Employment, New Delhi. The reference 
is answered accordingly. 


Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of 
India, New Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 17 दिसम्बर, 2025

No.L-20012/02/2017 (IR(CM-I)) dated 02/06/2017 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Sijua Area of M/s. BCCL, Sijua, Dhanbad and their workman through Secretary, 
Rashtriya Mazdoor Union, Sijua, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the action of the management of BCCL in awarding the punishment of termination to Shri 
Arjun Chauhan w.e.f. 21/1/1998 without conducting of fair enquiry is legal and justified, if not, to 
what relief the workman is entitled to and from which date?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-20012/02/2017 (IR(CM-D) dated 02/06/2017 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 09 of 2017 was registered on 12.06.2017 and

thereafter the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along

traceable after Covid-19 Pandemic. So case may be closed. Management has no objection.

withdrawn and it is further ordered that “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the above reference case. Let
